# Project Wiki — Eastsound WWTP Upgrade Phase I

Eastsound Sewer and Water District, Wastewater Treatment Plant Upgrade Phase I (Wilson Engineering job 2020-070). Public bid set; test bed only.

- **Version:** Merge rev1, 2026-09-29 (rev0 + Civil & Site resplit pass) · **Owner:** Merge thread · **Built from:** the five lane Wikis (Civil & Site, Process & Mechanical, Electrical & Controls, Structural & Building, Contract & General) and the index files 00, 01 (rev1), 02 (rev1), 03 (rev1), 04 (rev0).
- **Contents:** 202 notes — 97 drawing-sheet notes (96 cover-index sheets + C1.6A), 103 spec notes (95 sections + 8 appendices), 2 other units (Add. 4 generator exhibit, QA plan). Every crawlable unit in 01, 02 and 03 has exactly one note; no unit had notes from two lanes.
- **Order:** 01 sheet order (set pp.1–96, then the addendum-only sheet C1.6A as listed in 01), then 02 document order (sections and appendices), then other units in 00 register order (row 3 Add. 4, row 4 QA plan).
- **Notes are verbatim.** Each note body is copied byte-for-byte from its lane Wiki (checked by hash at build). Two Merge changes only: (1) every note heading is set to level 3 (Structural & Building wrote its notes at level 2); the heading text is unchanged; (2) one blockquote line under each heading gives the writing lane, the lane Issues file its "Issue" references point to, and the index position. That line is Merge metadata, not note content.
- **Resplit pass (rev1):** the Civil & Site notes for C0.3, C2.1, C2.3 and C2.6 come from the resplit pass on the Project-file images (Civil_and_Site_Resplit_Pass.md) and replace the Civil & Site "unavailable" placeholders.
- **Lane conventions matter.** Each lane wrote its own note format and citation shorthand (for example, Process & Mechanical makes the "Where it lives" line the default citation for its note). The lane preambles are reproduced verbatim in "Lane conventions" below; read a note with its lane's conventions.
- **Tags:** Verified = read in the stored text layer · Verified-Visual = read from the page image · Inferred = derived, basis stated · Unresolved = conflict or missing, need stated. Addenda supersede base documents (Add. 4 governs). WSDOT deferrals: "Unresolved: external reference, not staged."
- **Short names:** plans_N = `Pages_from_eswd-wwtp-upgrade-ph1-11x17-plans_N.pdf` · Add. 4 = `addendum-no4-eswd.pdf` · main spec = `eswd-wwtp-upgrade-phase-i-specs.pdf` · p. = PDF page within the named file.
- **Companion files:** Project_Ledger.csv / .xlsx (Ledger rows point to notes by unit ID in "Wiki Note(s)"); Exception_Report.md / .xlsx (cross-lane conflicts, gaps, PROPOSED tags for approval).

Conventions
- Tags: Verified = read in the stored text layer · Verified-Visual = read from the page image · Inferred = derived, basis stated · Unresolved = conflict or missing, need stated.
- Default citation: each note's "Where it lives" line is the citation for every claim in that note, at the default tag shown there (Verified-Visual where 01 sets Read Method = Visual; Verified for Text sheets and spec sections). Claims from another source, or with a weaker tag, carry their own bracketed citation and tag. Spec claims also cite ¶ and page inline.
- Addendum 4 governs over base documents; where an Add. 4 item touches a note, both are cited.
- WSDOT references are tagged "Unresolved: external reference, not staged." (03 decision F).
- "Related documents" links made by title or subject are Inferred unless the sheet or section cites them.
- Short names: plans_N = Pages_from_eswd-wwtp-upgrade-ph1-11x17-plans_N.pdf · Add. 4 = addendum-no4-eswd.pdf · main spec = eswd-wwtp-upgrade-phase-i-specs.pdf · p. = page within the named file. "Issue N" and "Open check N" refer to Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md.

### Electrical & Controls — preamble from Electrical_and_Controls_Wiki.md

#### Electrical & Controls — Project Wiki

One note per E sheet, Division 26 section, and the Add. 4 generator exhibit. Notes feed the Ledger (Electrical_and_Controls_Ledger.csv) through the "Wiki Note(s)" column.

- **Run status:** Complete (7 of 7 batches): all 35 E sheets, all 14 Division 26 sections, Add. 4 generator exhibit pp.11–13 — 50 notes.
- **Project:** Eastsound Sewer and Water District, WWTP Upgrade Phase I (Wilson Engineering job 2020-070). Public bid set; test bed only.
- **Lane:** Electrical & Controls. **Stack (03 Lane scopes):** all E sheets E0.1–E10.3 (35 sheets, all available); Division 26 in the main spec, 14 sections, pp.269–381; Add. 4 generator exhibit pp.11–13. Add. 4 items mapped to this lane in 03: Clarification 4 (p.1), Clarification 5 (p.1, exhibit pp.11–13), 26 80 00 ¶2.04 D (p.2); also-affects items 22 13 36 ¶2.01 A and 43 22 10 ¶2.01 A (p.2) and the A1.1/A1.2 drawing item (p.3).
- **Not read (other lanes or duplicates):** Division 26 file (duplicate), plans_7 pp.1–3 (duplicates), A1.1 reissue (Add. 4 p.4, Structural & Building stack), all non-E sheets, all non-Division 26 sections.
- **Tags:** Verified = read in the stored text layer · Verified-Visual = read from the page image · Inferred = derived, basis stated · Unresolved = conflict or missing, need stated.
- **Short names:** plans_N = `Pages_from_eswd-wwtp-upgrade-ph1-11x17-plans_N.pdf` · main spec = `eswd-wwtp-upgrade-phase-i-specs.pdf` · Add. 4 = `addendum-no4-eswd.pdf` · p. = PDF page within the named file. Spec page = 02 start page counted up with the section's "PAGE X OF Y" header (all 14 start pages reconcile with 02).
- **Rules applied:** Add. 4 governs over base documents (both cited) · body SECTION number governs over the running header · WSDOT deferrals tagged "Unresolved: external reference, not staged." · flags already raised in 01–04 are cited, not re-raised.

##### Ledger conventions used by this lane

- One row per item that gets built, bought, demolished, or connected: equipment, panels, instruments, fixture types, handholes, grounding systems, feeders that a key or demo note singles out as scope (e.g., P-TPS, P-SEC). Not carried: receptacles, switches, junction boxes, fittings, individual branch conduits (E6.3 is the conduit list; see Issues, workflow log).
- Existing equipment is carried only where new work demolishes, connects to, or modifies it.
- Equipment owned by another lane (blowers, pumps, fans, treatment packages) is carried because it shows on E sheets; Notes read "Cross-lane: <lane>". Merge de-duplicates.
- Tag: as printed. "(E)" is appended where the documents mark the item existing (E0.1: "E or (E) = existing device") and a new item carries the same tag. No printed tag: PROPOSED-<plain name>.
- Bid Item: per 04. Electrical work on E sheets and in Division 26 = Item 6 (00 24 13 ¶6 p.17, via 04). Other-lane equipment = Item 1 per 04's residual rule (Inferred) plus Item 6 for its power and control. Rows touching the Add. 4 temporary generator = "18 — Unresolved (see 04)". Permanent standby generator = unresolved Item 1 vs Item 6 (04, Other bid-item conflicts #1).
- Submittal / Testing columns: Y = a Division 26 section governing the item requires it, or for the temporary generator an Add. 4 requirement (paragraph cited in Source Citation); N = the governing Division 26 section was read and has no such requirement; Not stated = no Division 26 section governs the item (the governing spec is in another lane's stack); Pending = governing section not yet crawled in this run.
- Status: New / Existing / Demolished / Temporary / Not stated. Status taken from a printed "NEW", "(E)", "EXISTING" or demo note is Verified; status from context (e.g., equipment inside the new Blower Building) is Inferred with the basis in Notes.
- The row tag is the weakest tag of anything in the row.

### Structural & Building — preamble from Structural_and_Building_Wiki.md

#### Structural & Building — Project Wiki

Eastsound Sewer and Water District, WWTP Upgrade Phase I (Wilson Engineering job 2020-070). Public bid set; test bed only.

- **Lane:** Structural & Building. Stack per 03 Lane scopes: S and A sheets; Divisions 03, 05–09; 10 52 00; 13 12 20. 03 lists no "Other" items for this lane; 01 lists no unavailable sheets in it.
- **Units:** 9 sheets (01) plus Add. 4 reissue pages A1.1 (p.4), A1.2 (p.5), S2.3 (p.9), S4.1 (p.10); 16 spec sections, main spec pp.172–251 (02). All 16 PAGE X OF Y runs reconcile with 02 start/end pages (Verified).
- **Tags:** Verified = stored text layer · Verified-Visual = page image · Inferred = derived, basis stated · Unresolved = conflict or missing, need stated.
- **Rules applied:** Add. 4 (issued Feb 9, 2023) supersedes base documents — both cited and "Add. 4 governs" stated · body SECTION number governs over the running header · WSDOT deferrals tagged "Unresolved: external reference, not staged." · flags already in 01–04 are cited, not re-raised.
- **Y/N convention (assumption):** Submittal and Testing/Startup = Y when a spec section in this stack or the S1.1 structural notes require it (03 30 00 ¶1.05 A and 05 51 10 ¶1.04 A make the Drawing Structural Notes controlling); the source is cited in the row. Cross-lane items whose governing section is outside this stack stay "Not stated."
- **Short names (per 00):** plans_N = Pages_from_eswd-wwtp-upgrade-ph1-11x17-plans_N.pdf · Add. 4 = addendum-no4-eswd.pdf · main spec = eswd-wwtp-upgrade-phase-i-specs.pdf · p. = PDF page in the named file; spec p. = 02 start page counted up with the section PAGE X OF Y header.
- **Note format:** Document ID · Type · Discipline · Revision/Date · Summary (3–5 sentences, each cited and tagged) · Tags (equipment, spec sections, sheets, addenda) · Related documents; then one "Lives in" line.
- **Build status:** 25 of 25 notes (complete — all 25 units (9 sheets, 16 spec sections)). Pending: none.

### Contract & General — preamble from Contract_and_General_Wiki.md

#### Contract & General — Project Wiki (lane notes)

Eastsound Sewer and Water District, WWTP Upgrade Phase I (Wilson Engineering job 2020-070). Public bid set; test bed only.

- **Lane:** Contract & General · **Stack (03 "Lane scopes"):** no sheets; spec Divisions 00 and 01 (30 sections, per 02); Appendices D, E, I; QA plan (`cqa-plan-eswd-wwtp-ph1-2022-12-01.pdf`). Add. 4 read only where the 03 item map names this lane (Clarification 5, p.1).

- **Revision/Date:** spec sections carry no section-level dates; the project manual date (Dec. 30, 2022, cover p.1) comes from the 00 register.
- **Issues** are numbered in `Contract_and_General_Issues.md` (e.g., "Issues 1.1").

### G0.3 — Notes & Specifications

> Merge: lane — Civil & Site · Issue references → Civil_and_Site_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.3

G0.3 | Drawing sheet — general notes | General | BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022 (Verified); revision block not read (Text sheet) | The Contractor must not interrupt District operations, must verify locations and depths of existing utilities (shown for information only), must report dimension discrepancies to the Engineer, and must repair damage to existing improvements within 48 hours (General Notes 1, 3–6; plans_1 p.3, G0.3 — Verified). Trenches and excavations must meet federal OSHA requirements, and trench shoring design and installation are the Contractor's full responsibility (General Note 7; plans_1 p.3, G0.3 — Verified). A 1-week notice to the District is required before any utility service disruption, and the Contractor must provide temporary equipment (power generator, pumps, piping) and an approved plan for all WWTP disruptions; the note cites 'Section 01 11 00', whose body number is 01 11 10 (General Note 10; plans_1 p.3, G0.3 — Verified; alias already flagged, 02 conflict 1). The Operation Plan makes the Contractor responsible for sequencing so the existing WWTP is not interrupted and for all temporary bypass pumping, piping, holding tanks and storage (Operation Plan 1–3; plans_1 p.3, G0.3 — Verified). Survey monuments disturbed by construction need WA DNR permits under WAC 332-120 and replacement by a registered land surveyor at Contractor cost; earthwork, concrete and asphalt repair are to be prioritized in dry-weather months; County inspections (footings, concrete structures, electrical, final) and S1.1 special inspections need 24-hour notice to Owner and Engineer (Schedule, Survey Monumentation and Construction Inspections notes; plans_1 p.3, G0.3 — Verified). | Equipment: Temporary power generator; temporary bypass pumping, piping and holding tanks; Spec sections: 01 11 00 (cited; body 01 11 10); 00 73 00 Supplementary Conditions No. 7 (cited); Sheets: S1.1 (special inspections cited); Addenda: Add. 4 p.1, Clarification 5 (temporary generator) | Appendix F; C0.7 (unavailable); C0.5; S1.1; 01 11 10

### G0.5 — Process Schematic

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.5

Document ID: G0.5 | Type: Drawing — process schematic | Discipline: General (per 01, sheet prefix); process content | Revision/Date: BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022; revision "Ecology Review Comments" JGC 8/9/22 (triangle reads 6 at stored resolution)
Summary: 8" STEP influent and an influent sample sump feed the Influent Pump Station, which pumps through a 6" influent flow meter to the flow splitter; the splitter feeds Treatment Cell #1 (Train #1) on INF1, Treatment Cell #2 (Train #2) through an 8" Train #2 flow meter on INF2, and New Treatment Cell #3 (Train #3) through an 8" Train #3 flow meter on INF3, with a fourth outlet marked FUTURE. Secondary effluent SE1–SE3 passes an 8" effluent flow meter into UV Channels #1 and #2, then 10" final effluent runs to SSMH *1288 and the 12" outfall; a 4" UV chamber drain is shown. WAS1–WAS3 go to a WAS pump and the aerobic digester above the UV chamber; a digested sludge pump with a flow meter feeds the rotary fan press and the existing screw press (drawn gray), and the 4" pressate drain returns through a 4" plant drain flow meter to the pump station. Train #3 shows Anoxic 1, Anoxic 2, Aeration 1, Aeration 2 and a clarifier, with ML recycle airlift pumps, a RAS airlift pump, and a carbon addition point into Anoxic 2. Legend: isolation valve, flow direction, flow meter (FM), airlift pump.
Tags: Equipment — influent pump station, influent sample sump, flow splitter, flow meters (influent 6", Train #2 8", Train #3 8", effluent 8", plant drain 4", digested sludge), Train #3 zones and clarifier, RAS and ML recycle airlifts, carbon addition, UV channels #1–#2, aerobic digester, WAS pump, digested sludge pump, rotary fan press, existing screw press; line IDs INF1–INF3, SE1–SE3, WAS1–WAS3 · Spec sections — none printed · Sheets — none printed · Addenda — none
Related documents: G0.6, G0.7; C3.1–C3.5 (splitter, Train 3); C4.1 (Influent Pump Station); C5.1–C5.4 (UV and digester); C6.1–C6.2 (rotary fan press, sludge pumps); 46 53 00; 46 66 00; 46 76 26; 22 13 29 [links by title, Inferred]. Issue 18.

### G0.6 — Hydraulic Profile

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.6

Document ID: G0.6 | Type: Drawing — hydraulic profile, liquid and solids | Discipline: General (per 01); process content | Revision/Date: BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022; revisions "Add UV Disinfection" JGC 5/10/22 and "Ecology Review Comments" JGC 8/9/22
Summary: The liquid profile runs from the proposed influent sampling sump (FG 23.65) and the proposed influent & plant drain pump station (8" influent from the STEP collection system, IE 19.50; FG 23.30) through the plant influent flow meter to the proposed 3-way splitter box (HWL 28.0 at 0.77 MGD; bottom elev 26.00). Existing Trains #1 and #2 run at HWL 23.13 (0.257 MGD), NWL 23.09 (0.063 MGD), static 23.05; proposed Train #3 runs at HWL 25.38, NWL 25.34, static 25.30, top of concrete 26.80, with proposed Train #2 and Train #3 flow meters on the inlets. The proposed effluent flow meter feeds proposed UV disinfection channels #1 and #2 (HWL 21.49 at 0.50 MGD per channel; NWL 19.69 at 0.22 MGD per channel), then existing SSMH *1288 (rim 22.89) and 10" and 8" to the 12" outfall; the 100-year flood elevation is 13', and a proposed effluent sample sump is labeled. The solids profile shows proposed WAS pump equipment (pump rate 20–30 gpm), the proposed aerobic digester with a supernatant telescoping valve (TOC 26.80; supernatant to the plant drain P.S.), proposed sludge pump equipment (20–30 gpm), proposed rotary fan press dewatering equipment and the existing screw press, with filtrate to the plant drain P.S. and solids hauled off site.
Tags: Equipment — influent sampling sump, influent & plant drain pump station, 3-way splitter box, flow meters (plant influent, Train #2, Train #3, effluent), Trains #1–#3, UV channels #1–#2, effluent sample sump, WAS pumps, aerobic digester, supernatant telescoping valve, sludge pumps, rotary fan press, existing screw press · Spec sections — none printed · Sheets — none printed · Addenda — none
Related documents: G0.5, G0.7; C3.2 (splitter bottom 26.00); C3.4–C3.5 (Train 3 water levels match); C5.x; C6.x; 40 05 71 Telescoping Valve [by title, Inferred]. Issue 7; Open check 6, closed: C5.1 effluent sample chamber.

### G0.7 — Design Criteria

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.7

Document ID: G0.7 | Type: Drawing — design criteria tables | Discipline: General (per 01); process content | Revision/Date: BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022; revision block not captured
Summary: Start-up design (after Phase I) is ADF 0.10, MMF 0.12, max day 0.30 and peak hour 0.49 MGD; build-out (year 2040, after Phase II — phase numerals read from the image, Verified-Visual) is 0.19, 0.22, 0.46 and 0.77 MGD. The Influent Pump Station has 2 small submersible pumps (120–220 gpm each) and 2 large (320–500 gpm each), firm capacity 535 gpm (0.77 MGD) with one small and one large out of service; electromagnetic flow meters are influent 6" (0–880 gpm), Train #2 and Train #3 8" (0–470 gpm each), effluent 8" (0–940 gpm), plant drain 4" (0–200 gpm) and 2W water 1" (0–25 gpm). Train 3 has one 5 HP submersible mixer in each anoxic zone (#1 15,686 gal, #2 8,789 gal), aeration zone #1 (29,793 gal, 8 diffusers, 192 scfm) and zone #2 (9,889 gal, 2 diffusers, 48 scfm) with "medium bubble" aeration mixing, a RAS airlift (15 scfm, 3", 51 gpm) and a nitrate recycle airlift (61 scfm, 6", 203 gpm), for 316 scfm total at 9.2 psig; clarifiers are 3 units, 18 ft, SWD 13.25' (cells 1–2) and 15.50' (cell 3). UV has 2 lamps per module, 8 modules per channel and 2 channels at 0.5 MGD each, design dose printed "30,000 MJ/CM2" and UVT 50% or greater; the aerobic digester is 15,000 gal with coarse bubble aeration (6 diffusers × 20 cfm) and manual T-valve decant control. WAS and digester pumps are each 1 duty + 1 standby (swing) hose (peristaltic) pumps, 0–40 gpm; one rotary fan press takes 12 gpm at 2% or 20 gpm at 0.5% solids and makes 12–16% cake (up to 1,000 wet / 120 dry lb/hr); 2W water pumps are 1 duty + 1 standby submersible, 20–80 gpm on VFD control.
Tags: Equipment — influent pumps (2 small, 2 large), flow meters (6), Train 3 mixers, diffusers and airlifts, clarifiers, UV system, aerobic digester diffusers, WAS pumps, digester pumps, rotary fan press, 2W water pumps · Spec sections — none printed · Sheets — none printed · Addenda — none
Related documents: G0.5, G0.6; C3.3 (zone CF × 7.48 = G0.7 gallons, Inferred check); 46 53 00; 22 13 29; 22 13 36; 43 22 10; 46 41 23; 46 51 21; 46 66 00; 46 76 26 [by title, Inferred]. Anoxic table reads "TRAIN #4 (PROPOSED)" — Issue 1. Issues 2, 3, 4, 6; Issue 32.

Project description: total project area 0.45 ac, proposed impervious area 0.04 ac, area to be disturbed 0.30 ac, estimated cut 3,382 CY and fill 1,596 CY, stated as for the TESC narrative only and not for bidding or take-off (Item 15; plans_2Part-1 p.1, C0.2 — Verified-Visual). The site is mostly flat, with grass turf along the north and south property lines and no trees or shrubs; runoff flows southeast to northwest into a ditch on the adjoining site along the north boundary; there are no streams, lakes, wetlands, residential or critical areas; soils are mapped as Roche-Killebrew complex, 2 to 10 percent slopes (Items 16–21; plans_2Part-1 p.1, C0.2 — Verified-Visual). Sequence: install silt fence, construction entrance and inlet protection; remove pavement for sewer structures; clear and grade for the building addition and retaining wall; build them; install stormwater conveyance; pave and stabilize landscape areas (Item 22; plans_2Part-1 p.1, C0.2 — Verified-Visual). ### C0.5 — Demolition Plan

> Merge: lane — Civil & Site · Issue references → Civil_and_Site_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.12

C0.5 | Drawing sheet — demolition plan | Civil | BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022 (Verified); revision 1 'Ecology Review Comments' JGC 8/9/22 (Verified-Visual); Add. 4 p.1, Clarification 1 — Add. 4 governs | Demolition plan with 25 keyed notes covering removal of existing pipe (40 LF 8-in and 60 LF 10-in effluent/outfall, 40 LF 4-in roof drain, 87 LF storm drain with a catch basin, 20 LF 6-in drain, 60 LF 1-1/2-in sump force main, 7 ft and 8 LF of storm drain, and the 8-in influent pipe), 149 LF and 119 LF of chain link fence, 61 LF of retaining wall, and the generator fuel tank with its concrete spill basin (Keyed Notes 1–2, 8–9, 11–12, 16–21, 24; plans_2Part-4 p.1, C0.5 — Verified-Visual). Removals follow their replacements: the effluent pipes come out after the interim 8-in HDPE pipe (C0.7) is installed and tested, the sump pump station and its piping after the new plant drain pump station, and the septic tank (SSMH 1285) after the new septic tank and influent pump station, with temporary pumps, piping and basins as needed (Keyed Notes 1, 3–5, 8, 11–12; plans_2Part-4 p.1, C0.5 — Verified-Visual). The Contractor keeps the 2-in water service and sump station power live until replacements are tested, protects the storm drain pump station manhole (DMH 1287), potholes storm, power and telecom before new work, relocates existing holding tanks on site per Owner, and sawcuts asphalt pavement (Keyed Notes 5–6, 10, 13–15, 25; plans_2Part-4 p.1, C0.5 — Verified-Visual). Construction phasing and the demolition schedule cover the existing basin: remove the 8-in slab over the original pre-aeration basin by saw cut near the building's east face, remove fill while supporting internal piping, bypass all flow during a time-critical step, complete a checklist (walls, coatings, penetrations, three weir gates) before accepting wastewater, and remove internal piping after the new flow splitter is operational (Construction Phasing 1–5, Demolition Schedule 1–3; plans_2Part-4 p.1, C0.5 — Verified-Visual). A demolition plan with schedule must be approved by the Engineer before any demolition; Add. 4 states saw-cut information is on C0.5 but new surfacing information is on C2.6, which is unavailable — Add. 4 governs (General Note, plans_2Part-4 p.1, C0.5 — Verified-Visual; Add. 4 p.1, Clarification 1 — Verified). | Equipment: Existing: SSMH 1285 septic tank, SSMH 1286 sump pump station, DMH 1287 (protect), holding tanks, generator fuel tank and spill basin, chain link fence, retaining wall; Spec sections: 02 41 00 (related; not cited on sheet); Sheets: C0.1; C0.7 (unavailable); C1 site piping sheets; C2 storm drainage sheets; C2.6 (unavailable); C3.1; electrical sheets (all cited); Addenda: Add. 4 p.1, Clarification 1 | C0.1; C0.7 (unavailable); C1.3–C1.5; C2.6 (unavailable); C3.1 (Process & Mechanical); 02 41 00; Appendix F

Summary: Plan of the new flow splitter box beside existing Train #1, with 8" outlets to Train #1 (west), Train #2 and Train #3 (south), a future Train #4 connection sealed with an 8" blind flange (keyed note 1), and the inlet from the north; dashed vaults with flow meter symbols sit on the inlet line and on the Train #2 and Train #3 lines, and a vault drain is labeled. Keyed notes: proposed concrete support for the splitter box (2); existing sampling port (3); pipe supports per 5/C7.2 (4); carefully cut a hole through the building for the new 8" Train #1 influent and seal with flashing (5); 8" Train #1 influent elbow discharging into the aeration basin beside the existing influent pipe (7); confirm the existing Train #2 influent pipe and connect new 8" DI piping with positive drainage from the splitter box (8); pot-hole existing 8" piping before construction (9). Construction sequence: install the new splitter box and piping to Trains #1 and #3 without disturbing existing piping and operations; when complete and tested, connect the 8" influent to the new influent pump station and direct flow to Trains #1 and #3; then dewater, abandon and remove existing influent piping conflicts so the Train #2 piping and flow meter vault can be installed. Train #2 downtime is limited to 5 days max (Monday–Friday).

Summary: Keyed note 7 makes the flow splitter a prefabricated 316 SS unit sized to match the drawing, with 4 equal splitter chambers (flow to each of the 3 trains and 1 additional train), an adjustable weir in each chamber and a removable cover; manufacturer Smith & Loveless or approved equal. The inlet is 6" DI restrained pipe and a restrained 90° bend (INV 20.75) rising through a 12"×6" DI eccentric reducer, 12" DI flange spools and 90° bends on 12" pipe supports per 5/C7.2; outlets are 8" DI flanged bends and spools (one at INV 20.75) on 8" pipe supports per 5/C7.2, labeled To Cell #1, #2 and #3, with a future connection sealed by a blind flange (keyed note 1). A manually adjustable weir is configured to fine-tune an even split among all four outlet pipes.

### C4.1 — Influent Pump Station

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.31

Document ID: C4.1 | Type: Drawing — plan, elevation, keyed notes and control levels | Discipline: Civil (per 01); process-mechanical content | Revision/Date: BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022; revision "Ecology Review Comments" JGC 8/9/22
Summary: The new Influent Pump Station is a 96" precast wet well "per WSDOT Std Plan B-15.60-00 for precast manholes" (Unresolved: external reference, not staged) on a 12" thick precast base, interior bottom elev 9.0 and top elev 23.30, with the top slab per 1/S2.4 and 2/S2.4, a 36"×36" Halliday H1R3636 hatch and a 30"×48" Halliday H1R3048 hatch over the pumps (both H-20), and "WrapidSeal or equal" at all joints; it takes 8" influent at IE 19.50, 4" filtrate and digester supernatant at IE 13.40 and a 4" sample sump line at IE 18.00. Small and large pumps, each a duplex arrangement per specs (keyed notes 28, 52, 53), hang on stainless guide rails with stainless haul chains, manufacturer's disconnect elbows and flapper valves that close and seal when submerged; discharges pass through two precast valve vaults (4'-2" × 4'-2" × 5'-6" minimum inside, Utility Vault Co. 506-LA or equal, floor elev 18.0±) with 4" and 6" check and gate valves and pressure gages, into 4" and 6" DI restrained piping with buried gate valves. Floats F1–F4 on timer control start the redundant large lag (19.00), large lead (18.50), small lag (18.00) and small lead (17.50) pumps; the transducer, set 6" above the floor, runs T1 high level alarm 17.50 (with float), T2 large lag 17.00, T3 large lead 16.00, T4 small lag 14.00, T5 small lead 12.00, T6 pumps off 11.00 and T7 low level alarm and redundant off 10.50. Also shown: the influent sample sump structure (see 4/C7.5), a 2" SCH 80 conduit for the influent sampler intake tubing, a 3" stainless vent pipe with 24-mesh stainless screen (IE 21.00), clean outs, 2" PVC and 4" vault drains (IE 16.15), link-seal boots at every pipe-to-concrete penetration, 16 stainless anchor bolts, steps or ladder per WSDOT Std Plan B-30.90-01 (keyed note 21) and a 12" base of Class A gravel per WSDOT 9-03.12(1)A — both Unresolved: external reference, not staged. The vertical pipe brace points to detail 7/C7.2 (keyed note 27) and to "sheet M4.1" (elevation callout), which is not in the sheet index (Issue 11); Add. 4 p.1 Clarification 2 requires CANUSA-CPS WrapidSeal (Manhole Encapsulation System) or approved equal on the exterior of all precast concrete wet well and vault joints, installed per manufacturer, over the C4.1 call-out — Add. 4 governs.

### C4.2 — 2W Pump Station Plan & Profile

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.32

Document ID: C4.2 | Type: Drawing — plan and elevation | Discipline: Civil (per 01); process-mechanical content | Revision/Date: BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022; revision block blank
Summary: The plant water (2W) pump station is a 96" diameter precast wet well (floor 10.00', top 21.50', on a 12" crushed surfacing base course) under a 30" fiberglass wet well and access structure (top 0.2' above FG, 23.1'±) with an insulated fiberglass gasketed lid on stainless bolts and a 38" traffic-rated lid and manhole ring per 3/C7.5. 2W water arrives "from air gap, see C4.3" in 4" SCH 80 PVC at 2% slope (4" inlet IE 19.50; Kor-N-Seal or equal at the wall penetration), while the plan view labels a 6" inlet pipe (Issue 39). Two submersible well pumps per specifications (single-pump duty points 20 gpm @ 380' TDH, 72 gpm @ 150' TDH, 80 gpm @ 85' TDH) each discharge through a 2" high-pressure discharge assembly (ball valve, true union ball check valve, high-pressure flex hose, quick disconnect; Orenco or approved equal) set within 12" of the lid, with a pressure relief valve rated 110 psi or greater within 24" of the wet well top, then 2" SCH 80 PVC and a 2"×3" reducer to 3" HDPE; field-fit brackets steady the flow-induced tower assembly. Floats: F1 high level alarm and redundant valve close 22.00, F2 valve close 20.00, F3 valve open 19.00, F4 low level alarm and pumps off 15.00; a 1" PVC vent is tapped into the basin and run to a field-located point out of the vehicle drive path (coordinate with Owner). Add. 4 p.1 Clarification 2 (exterior of all precast concrete wet well joints sealed with CANUSA-CPS WrapidSeal or approved equal) applies to this precast wet well, where C4.2 calls no joint sealant — Add. 4 governs.

Document ID: C5.4 | Type: Drawing — UV unit plan, front, end and section views (not to scale), notes, interconnection table | Discipline: Civil (per 01); process-mechanical content | Revision/Date: BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022; revision "Add UV Disinfection" JGC 5-10-22
Summary: Detail 1 shows the Trojan UV disinfection unit: one bank (1A) of "4-64 UV PTP" modules, "6 required / bank" (read at stored resolution; the text layer also carries 6 module label groups), in a steel channel about 13'-7 1/2" long (a 9'-7" module section between 2'-0 3/4" transition boxes, 1'-4" inside channel height) with removable cover plates, module lamp status viewing plates, an access plate with warning label, solid blocking, 2" NPT drains with plugs, a standard 3600K level control weir with a 1" NPT drain plug, and flanged influent and effluent connections drilled to fit 12" DIN, BS and JIS pipe; HWL 21.49 upstream, 21.09 downstream, EL 19.08. Power: a splitter panel within 10 ft of the module power end (120 V, 1-phase, 19.1 A from distribution panel DP, not shown), power distribution receptacles (6.3 A each, from the splitter panel), 10' grounded power cords, and a wall-mounted system monitor within 15 ft of the channel (120 V, 5 A from DP). Notes: do not slope the channel floor; hold channel width and depth to ±1/4"; the contractor supplies all anchor bolts (3/8" [M10], 4 per transition box); electrical requirements cover the Trojan equipment only, with inrush per local code and extra outlets excluded from the interconnect amperage; review all Trojan installation instructions first; do not encase the steel channel in concrete. The module count and "4-64" label do not match G0.7's 8 modules per channel and 2 lamps per module (Issue 13).
Tags: Equipment — Trojan UV unit, UV PTP modules, level control weir, transition boxes, splitter panel, power distribution receptacles, system monitor · Spec sections — none printed · Sheets — none printed · Addenda — none

### C6.1 — Rotary Fan Press System

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.38

Document ID: C6.1 | Type: Drawing — plan, elevation and material list | Discipline: Civil (per 01); process-mechanical content | Revision/Date: BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022; revision "Ecology Review Comments" JGC 8/9/22
Summary: The material list names one each of: rotary press model (steel), flocculator (steel), sludge manifold and piping (316 SS), cake chute (LLDPE), air compressor and control panel, all "Section 46 76 26"; an emulsion polymer make-up system, "Section 46 33 33"; and screw conveyor equipment (SS), "Section 46 12 13" (not a section in the spec — Issue 14). The plan (press line about 161" [13'-5"] by 84 3/4" [7'-0 3/4"], conveyor 131 11/16" [10'-11 11/16"]) shows the press, flocculator, sludge manifold, operator interface, control panel, a 30"×30" "dry polymer mix unit" (Issue 16), 3" digested sludge in, 2" 2W water, and 4" pressate out "to influent pump station". The elevation shows the screw conveyor rising at 28° over 147 15/16" [12'-3 15/16"] to discharge at 90" [7'-6"] into a mobile dumpster for sludge cake collection (40" [3'-4"] high), with the 2W water supply pipe, 3" digester sludge and 4" pressate drain pipe below the floor.

### C6.2 — Sludge Pump and Valving Area

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.39

Document ID: C6.2 | Type: Drawing — equipment and piping plan | Discipline: Civil (per 01); process-mechanical content | Revision/Date: BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022; revision "Ecology Review Comments" JGC 8/9/22; title differs from the cover index ("& VALVING") — cosmetic, already logged in 01 (#8)
Summary: Three 3 HP packaged sludge pumps "per 22 13 36 packaged sludge pump equipment" (keyed note 8) sit side by side against an existing building exterior wall: a WAS pump, a swing pump (standby for either the WAS or the digested sludge pump) and a digested sludge pump. WAS #1, #2 and #3 each enter through an ASCO Redhat series 8221 2" normally closed solenoid valve (catalog 8221G013, 120 V coil, 150 psi max differential, brass body, stainless internals, NEMA 4X; keyed note 1) between 2" PVC manual ball valves (keyed note 7); WAS goes "to digester" in a 2" line carrying a 2" flow meter "per electrical design requirements" between 2" unions (keyed notes 2, 3). Digested sludge comes "from digester" in 4" SCH 80 PVC (keyed note 9) with a 4"×2" reducer, and the digested sludge pump discharges through a 2"×3" expansion "to rotary fan press"; manifold piping is 2" SCH 80 PVC (keyed note 4) on supports per 5/C7.2 (keyed note 5), with the wall annulus filled watertight with Dow Corning 732 sealant (keyed note 6). General note: coordinate piping alignment with the Owner and field configure for best access.
Tags: Equipment — WAS pump, swing pump, digested sludge pump (3 HP each), WAS solenoid valves (3), 2" flow meter, ball valves, unions, sludge manifold piping · Spec sections — 22 13 36 · Sheets — 5/C7.2 · Addenda — Add. 4 p.2, 22 13 36 ¶2.01 A (pump VFDs; applied on the pump rows)

Summary: Four biological treatment blowers stand on a common pad in the Blower Building, with 3" stainless steel air pipe (keyed note 1, "see exposed aeration piping, Section 33 31 00") and 6" stainless steel air pipe (keyed note 2, "see exposed & buried aeration piping, Section 33 31 00"); the digester blower of C6.3 sits north of the Blower Building wall, and 3" and 1" 2W pipes run along the west and south walls. The base sheet places two 2W bladder tanks inside the Blower Building near the south doors, fed by the 1" 2W pipe. Add. 4 p.6 (governs) adds red edits: increase the distance between the blower pad and the wall to 4 ft; increase the building separation to 9 inches maximum and keep the 10-ft setback from property lines; move the 2W bladder tanks and their 1" supply piping to the Train #2 Building (dimensioned 2.0' and 1.0'), configure as shown, field adjust as needed and coordinate with the Owner; route the 1" 2W pipe below the existing concrete floor and penetrate the slab at the bladder tank connection. Add. 4 p.3 adds that the Blower Building dimensions are adjusted, leaving about 9" to the existing Train 2 building while the new slab extends to the existing Train 2 slab, and that the MCC, MCP, bladder tanks and exhaust fans move (A1.1, A1.2, C6.4 item); Add. 4 p.1 Clarification 4 sets the Blower Building footprint at 9.25' × 44.00' (context; primary lane Electrical & Controls per 03).

The yard hydrant is a 1-in frostproof hydrant with a 1-in atmospheric vacuum breaker anchored to a post, and its callout points to 'Site Piping Plan, Sheet C2.2', which is the storm drainage sheet (Detail 1; plans_6 p.3, C7.1 — Verified-Visual; see Issues). The floor drain is a 4-in stainless steel drain with a 2-in trap and PVC outlet; the traffic-rated riser uses a 24-in cast iron frame and cover sealed with asphaltic sealant (Ramnek or equal), with a 12 x 8-in reinforced collar in traffic areas (Details 2–3; plans_6 p.3, C7.1 — Verified-Visual). Parking stalls are 9 ft minimum by 18'-6" with a van-accessible stall and 5-ft aisle; striping conforms to WSDOT 8-22 and SJCC 18.30.630(A)(4) (Detail 4; plans_6 p.3, C7.1 — Verified-Visual; WSDOT part Unresolved: external reference, not staged). Tracer wire is required on all new water piping, zip-strapped at 6-ft intervals and continuous, with locater boxes next to every building or structure the pipe enters; the Contractor performs a conductivity/locate test on completion and final box locations go to the Engineer (Detail 6 Notes 1–6; plans_6 p.3, C7.1 — Verified-Visual). | Equipment: Yard hydrant; floor drain; traffic-rated riser and lid; parking striping; gravity sewer cleanout; tracer wire and locater boxes; Spec sections: None cited on sheet; Sheets: C2.2 (cited by Detail 1); C7.5 (cites 3/C7.1); C7.10 (cites 2/C7.1); Addenda: — | C1.3 (Pipe ID 22); C7.5; C7.10; 33 32 00

C7.10 | Drawing sheet — flow meter vault details | Civil | BID SET; title block NOV 2, 2022; seal 11-02-2022 (Verified); revision 1 'Ecology Review Comments' JGC 8/9/22 (Verified-Visual); Add. 4 p.1, Clarification 2 applies (precast vault joints) — Add. 4 governs | Plan and elevation of the Train 2 (Detail 1) and Train 3 (Detail 2) flow meter vaults, each a precast concrete vault 4'-2" x 4'-2" x 5'-0" minimum inside (Utility Vault 506-LA or equal) with a 36 x 36-in H-20 single-leaf hatch (Halliday H1R3636) opening south and a Bilco LadderUp (plans_7 p.4, C7.10 — Verified; details Verified-Visual). Each vault houses an 8-in flow meter with an 8-in flanged coupling adapter with restraints, with 8-in plug valves in valve cans upstream and downstream; the pipe is labeled 8-in DI force main in plan and 8-in DI gravity on the elevation (plans_7 p.4, C7.10 — Verified-Visual; see Issues). Pipe inverts are 20.72 (Train 2) and 20.68 (Train 3) over a nominal floor at 19.0 with 1% grout slope to a floor drain (2/C7.1) that drains to the influent pump station; pipe supports follow 5/C7.2 with 18 in minimum clearance (plans_7 p.4, C7.10 — Verified-Visual). Pipe penetrations get Link-Seal boots, steps or ladder must be non-corrosive per WSDOT Standard Plan B-30.90-02 (galvanized not accepted), and the base is 12 in of Class A foundation gravel per Standard Specifications 9-03.12(1)A (plans_7 p.4, C7.10 — Verified-Visual; WSDOT parts Unresolved: external reference, not staged). Add. 4 Clarification 2 requires the exterior of all precast vault joints to be sealed with CANUSA-CPS WrapidSeal or approved equal — Add. 4 governs (Add. 4 p.1, Clarification 2 — Verified). | Equipment: Train 2 and Train 3 flow meter vaults; 8-in flow meters; 8-in plug valves; Spec sections: None cited on sheet; Sheets: C7.1 (Detail 2); C7.2 (Detail 5); C7.9 (unavailable, same title); Addenda: Add. 4 p.1, Clarification 2 | C1.4 (Valves 2, 3); C1.3 (Pipe IDs 8, 11, 13); C7.9 (unavailable); 03 40 00 (Structural & Building); 26 80 00 (Electrical & Controls)

### S1.1 — Structural Notes

> Merge: lane — Structural & Building · Issue references → Structural_and_Building_Issues.md · index — 01 set p.56

- **Document ID:** S1.1
- **Type:** Drawing — structural general notes: design criteria, foundations, concrete, anchorage, steel, submittals, special inspections
- **Discipline:** Structural (Kingworks Structural Engineers, KW job 21131, for Wilson 2020-070)
- **Revision/Date:** Title block 10-15-2021; revision 2 "Bid Set" TH 9/6/22 [plans_7 p.8 — Verified-Visual]. No Add. 4 entry. Page total reads "OF 98" — logged in 01 (#10), not re-raised.
- **Summary:** Design basis is the 2018 IBC, Risk Category III, 100 psf floor live load, 25 psf ground and flat-roof snow (Ct 1.1, Ce 1.1, Is 1.1), 105 mph ultimate wind (Exposure C), and seismic Site Class D (default), SDC D, Ie 1.25, SDS 0.79g, SD1 0.51g [plans_7 p.8, S1.1 General Notes 1–6 — Verified-Visual]. Foundations use 2,500 psf allowable bearing (×1.33 for wind or seismic), a minimum 1'-6" frost cover, and the GeoEngineers report dated September 7, 2021 (clouded, revision 2) for subgrade, backfill and compaction [plans_7 p.8, Foundations & Subgrade 1, 2, 8 — Verified-Visual; report-date conflict already flagged in 02 Unresolved spec items #2 and the 03 Merge flag — cited, not re-raised]. The concrete material schedule sets 4,500 psi and 0.45 max w/cm for three groups: Blower Building slab-on-grade, grade beams and misc. equipment pads (3/4" aggregate, 6% air, 30% min fly ash/slag); hydraulic structures (1" aggregate, air per notes, 20%); and the "Drain Pump Station Lid" (3/4", 5%, 20%) [plans_7 p.8, Reinforced Concrete 2 — Verified-Visual]. Minimum structural shop drawings are concrete reinforcing (including wall elevations), mix designs, a slab-on-grade jointing plan and structural steel; deferred submittals, sealed by a Washington PE, are metal stairs & landings and the pre-engineered metal building for the Blower Building [plans_7 p.8, Submittals 4–5 — Verified-Visual]. Structural steel follows AISC 303-16 with A992 W/WT, A500 Gr B HSS, A53 Gr B pipe, A325 bolts, F1554 Gr 36 anchor rods, A36 misc. steel and E70XX welds, exterior steel hot-dip galvanized; a structural special inspection schedule covers steel, concrete, post-installed anchors, and soils and foundations [plans_7 p.8, Structural Steel 1–4 and Special Inspection schedule — Verified-Visual].
- **Tags:** Design criteria; concrete material schedule; special inspection schedule; deferred submittals (metal stairs & landings; Blower Building metal building); "Drain Pump Station Lid"; hydraulic structures · Spec (by subject, Inferred): 03 30 00, 05 50 00, 05 51 10, 13 12 20 · Sheets: S2.1, S2.2, S2.3, S2.4, S4.1 · Addenda: none

Detail 4 requires a hydrophilic waterstop (submit for approval), a grout surface per spec, and subgrade preparation per the structural notes and geotech report, and shows "guardrail, mechanical equipment, and ladders per civil" [plans_8 p.1, Detail 4 — Verified-Visual]. Horizontal laps may align vertically only at every third bar or more, with lap length per 3/S4.1 [plans_8 p.1, Detail 5 — Verified-Visual]; the S4.1 table covers Grade 60 uncoated bars only while these horizontal bars are Grade 75 [plans_8 p.5; Add. 4 p.10, S4.1 Detail 3 Note 1 — Verified-Visual; Unresolved: need Grade 75 lap lengths — Issues, Conflicts #2].

The flow splitter sits on a new concrete pad on the existing equipment slab-on-grade, reinforced #4 @ 12" o.c. each way top and bottom with #4 hooked dowels @ 24" o.c. drilled and epoxied around the perimeter and a roughened joint; flow splitter per civil, pad per mech and civil, attachment by others [plans_8 p.4, Detail 5 — Verified-Visual].

- **Summary:** Details 1–7 are typical concrete details: wall-to-mat-slab reinforcing (hydrophilic waterstop for hydraulic structures, submit for approval), wall corner and intersection reinforcing, development and lap splice lengths, standard hooks, beam stirrups, and small and large penetrations [plans_8 p.5; Add. 4 p.10 — Verified-Visual]. The development and splice table is for 4,500 psi concrete (for #5: Ld 28"/22" top/other, Ls 36"/28"), and its Note 1 limits the values to Grade 60 uncoated bars [plans_8 p.5; Add. 4 p.10, Detail 3 — Verified-Visual]. Add. 4 adds Detail 8, a trench in the existing slab for sub-floor pipe: saw-cut the existing slab (24" max.), encase the new pipe in CDF (1,000 psi min.), infill to match the existing slab thickness with #4 dowels @ 18" o.c. alternating sides, drilled and epoxied with 4" min. embedment, and sleeve per 6/S2.3 without coring through rebar where pipe passes under or through an existing footing [Add. 4 p.10, Detail 8 — Verified-Visual; Add. 4 governs]. Add. 4's C1.6A item sends floor repair at the sludge piping cuts to Detail 8, matching the Blower Building slab concrete and reinforcement, with new rebar doweled and epoxied at least 4" into existing concrete [Add. 4 p.3, Drawings C1.6A — Verified; Add. 4 governs].
- **Tags:** Details: wall-to-mat reinforcing and waterstop, corners and intersections, development and splice lengths (4,500 psi, Grade 60), standard hooks, stirrups, penetrations, trench in existing slab (Detail 8) · Spec (by subject, Inferred): 03 30 00 · Sheets: S2.1 (3/S4.1), S2.2 (6/S4.1, 7/S4.1), S2.3 (6/S2.3); cross-lane C1.6A · Addenda: Add. 4 p.3 (Drawings S2.3/S4.1; C1.6A item cites Detail 8); p.10 reissue
- **Related documents:** S2.1; S2.2; S2.3; S1.1; 03 30 00; cross-lane C1.6A (Civil & Site); Add. 4 pp.3, 10

The panel is the Treatment Building unit (gas alarms, fans 1–3, IPS PLC points on E8.3/E8.4), but LP2 circuit 5 feeds the ventilation control panel on E6.2 and E4.1 — see Issues (plans_12 p.5; plans_10 p.3; plans_9 p.6) [Verified; unit identity Inferred]. | Treatment Building ventilation control panel; NEMA 4X SS; H2S alarm; methane alarm; ventilation fans 1–3 (EF-3 to EF-5); alarm horn; CR1–CR3; LP1 vs LP2; E4.1; E6.2; E8.3; E8.4; 26 80 00 | E4.1 (key notes 3 and 7); E6.2 (LP2 circuit 5); E8.3; E8.4; E2.2 (Blower Building ventilation controls, separate panel); 23 34 00 (Process & Mechanical stack); 26 80 00 |

| Summary | Bidders must be registered with WA Labor & Industries, corporations with the WA Secretary of State, and be regularly employed in this type of work (¶1, p.11; Verified). Interpretations and changes bind only by Addendum, substitutions are not considered before bid receipt, and bidders must acknowledge addenda in the bid (¶3.C–D, ¶4.B, p.11; Verified). Subcontractors individually over 10% of the Contract Sum are listed with the bid or within one hour of bid time, and bid security is 5% of the total bid (¶5.C, ¶7.A, p.12; Verified). Award goes to the lowest responsible responsive bidder within 45 days of opening, in any combination of bid schedules and alternates; the contract bond is the full contract amount and stays in effect until one year after Substantial Completion (¶12–14, pp.13–14; Verified). The awardee returns the signed contract within 10 working days of award (¶15.A) but ¶16.A says 10 calendar days (p.14; Issues 1.3, Unresolved); no work on site before Notice to Proceed (¶15.C, p.14; Verified). |

### 00 31 13 — Preliminary Project Phase

> Merge: lane — Contract & General · Issue references → Contract_and_General_Issues.md · index — 02 row 8

| Summary | The phasing plan is for planning only and does not dictate means and methods (p.21; Verified). San Juan County building permit applications were submitted Nov 2021 with approvals June 2022; bid phase: advertise Jan 4, pre-bid Jan 18, open bids Feb 8, award Feb 15, preconstruction meeting Mar 15, 2023 (p.21; Verified). Construction starts with submittals after award (long-lead items first) and March 2023 mobilization, a temporary bypass pipe per the Plan of Interim Operation (April 2023), then Blower Building, digester, UV disinfection chamber, sludge pumps, yard piping, vaults and electrical (April 2023), the 2W air gap water system (May 2023), influent pump station (June 2023) and Train 3 (August 2023) (¶A.1–10, p.21; Verified). A temporary 480V generator is rented from Sept 25, 2023 until the permanent 480V generator is installed, tested and on line in April 2024, and the new 480V service, MCC and PLC panel complete Oct 4, 2023 (¶A.11–12, p.21; ¶A.21, p.22; Verified). Milestones: major piping Nov 1, 2023; dewatering system Feb 9, 2024; Train 3 complete July 8, 2024 then seeded with 10,000 gal of activated sludge July 8–12; Substantial Completion July 26, 2024; Final Completion Oct 16, 2024 (¶A.18–27, p.22; Verified). |

### 00 73 00 — Supplementary Conditions

> Merge: lane — Contract & General · Issue references → Contract_and_General_Issues.md · index — 02 row 25

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Document ID | 00 73 00 |

| Discipline | General |
| Revision/Date | Project manual Dec. 30, 2022 (main spec cover p.1, per 00 register); pp.98–121 carry the Ecology WPCRF insert "Revised 1/22/21" |
| Summary | Thirty-five Supplemental Conditions modify the WSDOT Standard Specifications (2022 Ed.), which serve as the base conditions (Unresolved: external reference, not staged); precedence runs Change Orders, Contract Form, Addenda, permits, Drawings, Supplemental Conditions, Technical Specifications, District standards, WSDOT drawings, WSDOT specs (SC 1–2, 5, pp.88–89; Verified); the 00 53 00 and 00 54 00 funding inserts are not placed in that order (Issues 2.7). The Contractor stakes the work, picks up the District Land Disturbance permit and San Juan County Building Permit #21-0385 (stated as still in process), gives 48-hour utility and connection notices, works 7AM–5PM Mon–Fri with $150/hr inspection charge past 40 hr/week, and self-performs at least 50% of the contract price (SC 7–14, pp.89–91; Verified; permit status conflicts with 00 31 13 — Issues 1.9). SC 15 sets completion by Oct. 16, 2024 within 360 working days on site; SC 16 requires the CPM schedule at the pre-construction meeting and the Schedule of Values within 10 working days of NTP; SC 17 holds 5% retainage; SC 18 sets liquidated damages per working day as LD = 0.15C/T per WSDOT 1-08.9 (pp.91–92; Verified; WSDOT part Unresolved: external reference, not staged; basis conflicts with 00 41 00 — Issues 1.8). SC 19 defines Substantial Completion as the new facility in beneficial use (influent pump station, flow splitter, Train 3, digester, dewatering, blower building, UV, yard piping, electrical, instruments, controls, backup generator, SCADA/PLC programming) and Final Completion as including removal of temporary pump/process equipment, site work, paving, performance testing and cleanup (p.92; Verified). Red-line record drawings before 90% payment, temporary barriers at all hazards, insurance ($1M/$2M bodily injury, $1M property damage, builders risk equal to the bid, Owner and Engineer additional insured), one-year guarantee (start date conflicts — Issues 1.11), and funding clauses follow (SC 20–35, pp.92–97; Verified); pp.98–121 repeat the 00 53 00 Ecology insert word for word (Issues 1.13). |

### 01 45 00 — Quality Control

> Merge: lane — Contract & General · Issue references → Contract_and_General_Issues.md · index — 02 row 31

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Document ID | 01 45 00 |
| Type | Spec section — Division 01 General Requirements (Part 3) |
| Discipline | General |
| Revision/Date | Project manual Dec. 30, 2022 (main spec cover p.1, per 00 register); no section-level date |
| Summary | Splits inspection and testing between the parties: the Contractor keeps records of all inspections and tests, pays for repeat tests after any failure (including Owner-responsibility tests), gives 48 hours' notice before work needing inspection, and uses only Owner-approved laboratories (¶1.05.A–E, ¶1.06.C, pp.137–139; Verified). Contractor-paid: pipe pressure testing and potable water pipe disinfection per the technical sections, equipment testing per the equipment sections (Division 46, p.141), clean water leakage testing, and clean water mechanical testing of pumps, diffusers, mixers and air lift assemblies (¶1.05.B.1, p.137; Verified). Owner-paid, Contractor-coordinated: building special inspections for concrete and structural steel (the p.141 summary adds air barrier testing per WA Energy Code Section 402), earthwork, concrete, asphalt (Rice density, compaction, extraction/gradation), coatings and field welding (¶1.05.B.2, pp.137–138, p.141; Verified); the WSDOT/AASHTO compaction and asphalt testing standards (¶1.03.B, p.136) are Unresolved: external reference, not staged. Before startup, eight new structures get a fill-to-rim clean water leakage test held 2 hours with no visible leakage — influent pump station, flow splitter, aeration basins, anoxic basins, clarifier, UV disinfection chamber, digester, 2W water system pump station (¶1.09, pp.139–140; Verified; repeated in 01 50 00 — Issues 1.22). The Owner's on-site representative cannot change the contract or accept work, and missed defects can still be rejected later (¶1.07–1.08, p.139; Verified). |

### 01 50 00 — Temporary Facilities

> Merge: lane — Contract & General · Issue references → Contract_and_General_Issues.md · index — 02 row 32

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Document ID | 01 50 00 |
| Type | Spec section — Division 01 General Requirements (Part 3) |
| Discipline | General |
| Revision/Date | Project manual Dec. 30, 2022 (main spec cover p.1, per 00 register); no section-level date |
| Summary | The Contractor arranges all temporary utilities; a field office is encouraged but not required, while water and sanitation must be provided for all workers (¶1.01.A–B, p.142; Verified; field office conflicts with 01 31 00 — Issues 1.16). Required facilities: temporary power and lighting to the closest utility source, metered construction water with backflow prevention if agreed, temporary restrooms, drinking water, protected storage, first aid per WAC 296-24, and access at all times to homes, the WWTP and water treatment plant, and the helicopter pad for emergency vehicles (¶1.04.A–G, I, pp.142–144; Verified). Wastewater bypass systems are required for both influent and effluent; bypass pumping and other temporary process equipment need continuous oversight by on-site staff or an alarm with automatic cellular notification, 24/7 call-out with on-site response within 30 minutes, and backup alarms and running status wired to the District's existing control system, with PLC/SCADA programming incidental (¶1.01.D.2, ¶1.04.H, pp.142–144; Verified). The clean water leakage test repeats 01 45 00 but groups the aeration basins, anoxic basins and clarifier under Train #3 and names UV channels #1 and #2 and the 2W pump station wet well (¶1.04.C, p.143; Verified; Issues 1.22). Cleanup, noise, dust, erosion control per 31 32 11, pollution control and barriers at all hazards close the section (¶1.05–1.08, pp.144–145; Verified; barrier text repeats 00 73 00 SC 23), and temporary items come out at completion of the work (¶1.01.C, ¶1.04.A.4, ¶1.04.B.3, ¶1.04.D.1; removal timing conflicts with 01 70 00 — Issues 1.19). |

### 01 60 00 — Product Requirements

| Summary | All material and equipment must be new, from established manufacturers, complete with accessories and guards, and installed per the manufacturer's printed instructions, with one instruction set kept on site and any conflict reported to the Owner (¶1.01.C, ¶1.03, p.146; Verified). Products arrive in labeled original containers, are inspected on delivery, are accepted only when unloading forces are on site, and are stored per the manufacturer with the Contractor fully responsible (¶1.04–1.05, pp.146–147; Verified). Named products set a quality standard; substitutes need the Owner's written acceptance before ordering, and requests are considered for 30 days after the Contract Date (¶1.06.A.4, ¶1.06.B.1, p.147; Verified; conflicts with the 10-day deadline in 01 33 00 — Issues 1.17). Each request goes in separately, six copies, with comparison data, schedule and cost effects; the Contractor pays the Engineer's review and redesign costs, and the Owner answers within two weeks (¶1.06.B.2–C, pp.147–148; Verified). Products likely to need later replacement must be standard, domestically produced items (¶1.06.A.2, p.147; Verified). |

### 01 70 00 — Execution and Closeout Requirements

> Merge: lane — Contract & General · Issue references → Contract_and_General_Issues.md · index — 02 row 35

| Field | Value |
|---|---|

| Discipline | General |
| Revision/Date | Project manual Dec. 30, 2022 (main spec cover p.1, per 00 register); no section-level date |
| Summary | Before requesting Substantial Completion the Contractor delivers tools, spare parts and extra stock, changes over locks and hands over keys, completes start-up testing and operator instruction, removes temporary facilities, turns over record information, finishes cleanup, and provides O&M manuals for all equipment, controls, telemetry and SCADA plus 2015 WSEC compliance documentation per C103.6.3 (¶1.03.A, pp.152–153; Verified; temporary facility removal conflicts with 00 73 00 SC 19 — Issues 1.19; energy code edition — Issues 1.20). The request carries a completion notice, incomplete-items list, pay request, change accounting, warranties and bonds, releases and occupancy/operating certificates, and record documents; "substantially complete" is defined by the WSDOT General Specifications and "Sections 00 72 00 and 00 73 00a" (¶1.03.B, p.153; WSDOT part Unresolved: external reference, not staged; section numbers — Issues 2.5), and warranties start on the Date of Substantial Completion (¶1.03.D.1, p.153; Verified; bears on Issues 1.11). Retainage is released only against an affidavit of paid payrolls and bills, lien releases, the DOR tax form, the L&I affidavit of wages paid and the ESD release (¶1.05.B, p.154; Verified). All mechanical and electrical equipment is tested before any facility goes into operation: procedures due 14 days before start-up covering every control device, equipment item and control circuit, joint sign-off forms, testing witnessed by the Engineer, and manufacturers' representatives who train Owner staff and certify each installation by letter (¶1.06, p.155; Verified). A daily record print set and a marked-up project manual are kept on site, then final cleanup (¶1.07–1.10, pp.155–156; Verified). |

Paragraph numbers jump from 1.07 to 1.10 and ¶1.11 starts at "B" (p.159; Issues 2.8). > Merge: lane — Structural & Building · Issue references → Structural_and_Building_Issues.md · index — 02 row 40

- **Document ID:** 03 30 00
- **Type:** Specification section — Division 03
- **Discipline:** Structural
- **Revision/Date:** Main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (00 register: cover p.1, Engineer's Statement seal p.3); no revision marks on the section pages; no Add. 4 item [main spec pp.172–180 — Verified].
- **Summary:** Covers labor, materials and equipment for subgrade preparation, forms, rebar and placement of all cast-in-place concrete shown, paid as incidental to the lump-sum bid items in which the work appears; the only extra-work items are overexcavation and structural fill under 31 20 00, by written authorization [¶1.01 A.1, ¶1.03, p.172 — Verified]. The Drawing Structural Notes take precedence over less specific spec requirements and set the mix designs and reinforcing, while WSDOT M41-10 (2022), WSDOT material sources, WSDOT-approved aggregate sources and WSDOT 9-04.2 joint sealant are incorporated by reference [¶1.05 A, ¶2.03 A, ¶2.04 B–C, pp.172–175 — Verified; ¶1.04 A.1, ¶1.06 D.4, ¶2.04 A, ¶2.04 D — Unresolved: external reference, not staged]. Submittals are ACI SP-66 shop drawings, mix designs 15 working days before use, material certificates, a PE-prepared hot and cold weather plan, joint layout plans (control joints no more than 30× slab thickness where not shown), and WA SE-sealed formwork plans and calculations for the "Clarifier, digester and chlorine contact basin structures" — names that do not all match the sheet titles [¶1.06 A–G, p.173 — Verified; Issues, Missing information]. The Owner provides independent testing and inspection (subgrade compaction and moisture, reinforcing placement, concrete placement and cylinders) and special inspections, and anchor bolts are set to AISC 303-16 §7.5 tolerances by a registered land surveyor [¶1.07 B, p.174; ¶3.012 A, p.180; ¶3.03 B, p.176 — Verified]. Products and execution include hydrophilic waterstop (Cetco RX-101T or Greenstreak Hydrotite CJ0725, 3" min. cover), 15-mil Stego Wrap vapor retarder, 40-diameter laps unless shown otherwise, steel-trowel interior slabs damp-cured only with 1/8"/ft grout slopes to sumps, and a moist cure of 90 days min. for all structural concrete against 7 days for building slabs [¶2.04 F, ¶2.05, ¶3.05 B, ¶3.09 A.1, ¶3.010 D, pp.175–179 — Verified; cure duration — Unresolved, Issues, Conflicts].

### 03 40 00 — Precast Concrete

> Merge: lane — Structural & Building · Issue references → Structural_and_Building_Issues.md · index — 02 row 41

- **Document ID:** 03 40 00
- **Type:** Specification section — Division 03
- **Discipline:** Structural
- **Revision/Date:** Main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (00 register: cover p.1, Engineer's Statement seal p.3); no revision marks on the section pages. Add. 4 p.1 Clarification 2 extends exterior joint sealing to all precast wet well and vault joints — Add. 4 governs [main spec pp.181–185; Add. 4 p.1 — Verified].
- **Summary:** Covers design, supply and installation of all precast concrete structures shown — vaults, top slabs and "the effluent wet well" — with WA-engineer-sealed shop drawings and calculations, mix designs 15 days ahead, material certificates, 5 years of NPCA certification, and Owner/Engineer plant inspection rights [¶1.01 A, p.181; ¶1.06 A–F, ¶1.07, p.182 — Verified; WSDOT M41-10 references ¶1.04 C and ¶1.05 B — Unresolved: external reference, not staged]. Wet wells and vaults are 5,000 psi with A615 Gr 60 rebar to ACI 318-14 and ASTM C857: 96" Oldcastle wet wells with 48"×30" H-20 aluminum hatches (LW Products HD-3C) at the 2W Plant Water Pump Station (C4.2) and Influent Pump Station (C4.1), and 4'×4' Oldcastle 4686-LA vaults with 36"×36" H-20 single-leaf hatches (Halliday H1R3636) for the Influent and Effluent Flow Meter Valve Vaults and Plants 2 & 3 Flow Meter Vault (C7.4) and the Influent Pump Station Meter Vaults (C4.1) [¶2.02 E, p.183 — Verified]. The top slab schedule separately calls for the Influent Pump Station top slab per the Contract Plan details with a new 3'-0"×5'-0" light-duty aluminum hatch with safety grates (Flygt FLED-13 or equal), which conflicts with the 48"×30" H-20 hatch in the vault table [¶2.02 D.1 and E.2, p.183 — Verified; Unresolved — Issues, Conflicts]. Accessories are non-shrink grout (6,000 psi at 7 days) for anchor bolt and handrail pockets and non-mechanical base plates, 1:2 Portland cement grout (4,000 psi) for wall-slab slopes, ASTM C881 Type IV epoxy grout for pump bases, RAM-NEK or Quikseal sealant gaskets, and Canusa WrapidSeal heat-shrink external joint sleeves [¶2.02 F–I, p.184 — Verified], which Add. 4 applies to the exterior of all precast wet well and vault joints [Add. 4 p.1, Clarification 2 — Verified; Add. 4 governs]. Installation requires watertight gasketed joints, primed heat-shrink sleeves with 3 days' notice by a manufacturer-trained installer, backfill compacted to 95% within 4 ft of vault walls, frames set within 1/4" vertical, and hatches machined for a solid fit [¶3.02 B–G, p.185 — Verified].
- **Tags:** Structures: 2W Plant Water Pump Station wet well; Influent Pump Station wet well and top slab; Influent Flow Meter Valve Vault; Effluent Flow Meter Valve Vault; Plants 2 & 3 Flow Meter Vault; Influent Pump Station Meter Vaults; "effluent wet well"; hatches (LW HD-3C, Halliday H1R3636, Flygt FLED-13); joint sleeves; grouts · Spec: 03 30 00, 31 20 00 · Sheets: S2.4 (drain pump station wet well, by subject — Inferred); cross-lane C4.1, C4.2, C7.4 · Addenda: Add. 4 p.1 Clarification 2

Submittals are shop drawings for all fabricated items, product data, nosing samples, PE qualification data, stainless mill certificates, paint compatibility certificates and welder certificates, with welding to AWS D1.1/D1.6 by state-licensed welders [¶1.04, p.187; ¶1.05 B, p.187; ¶2.04 A, p.192 — Verified]. Ferrous metal not fully embedded in concrete is hot-dip galvanized after fabrication (2 oz/sf min.), aluminum touching concrete gets bituminous paint, and all-thread hanger rod is allowed only in stainless [¶2.02 D, p.191; ¶2.010, p.193; ¶3.04 A, p.197; ¶2.08, p.193 — Verified]. Mechanical anchors are limited to non-water-bearing concrete (building slabs, walkways, Engineer-approved locations), and chemical anchors are required on all water-bearing structures and wherever spacing or edge distance is under 3.5" [¶2.03 B, pp.191–192 — Verified]. Handrails and guardrails are specified here as 1-1/4" steel pipe (ASTM A53 Gr B) while ¶2.02 B specifies aluminum pipe railings, and grouting is referred to a Section 04 60 00 that exists neither in the main spec nor in 02 [¶2.014 A, p.196; ¶2.02 B.1, p.191; ¶3.03 E, p.197 — Verified; Unresolved — Issues].

Submittals are shop drawings with a dimensional layout, the manufacturer's assembly and installation instructions, and calculations sealed by a Washington State registered structural engineer; partial or unsealed submittals are rejected [¶1.02 A–B, p.203 — Verified]. Rails, posts and stanchions are 1-1/2" IPS Schedule 40 aluminum with a clear satin anodized Class I finish (0.7 mil min.) from Golden Railing, CRL or Ametco or equal, with 3/16" min. aluminum toeboards on all guardrails [Part 2 A–B, p.203 — Verified]. Connections to structure use Type 316 stainless bolts, posts and rails get weep holes, removable sections mount independently, aluminum touching concrete gets bituminous paint, and embedded posts are grouted into blockouts after the concrete reaches design strength [¶3.01 B–D, ¶3.02 A–B, p.204 — Verified].
- **Tags:** Items: handrails and guardrails (locations per civil drawings) · Spec: 05 50 00 (conflicting steel railing, ¶2.014 A), 05 51 10 · Sheets: guardrail "per civil" on S2.1, S2.2 · Addenda: none

### 05 53 00 — Grating

> Merge: lane — Structural & Building · Issue references → Structural_and_Building_Issues.md · index — 02 row 45

- **Document ID:** 05 53 00
- **Type:** Specification section — Division 05
- **Discipline:** Structural
- **Revision/Date:** Main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (00 register: cover p.1, Engineer's Statement seal p.3); no revision marks on the section pages; no Add. 4 item [main spec pp.205–206 — Verified].
- **Summary:** Scope is aluminum grating set in pultruded vinyl ester FRP embedment angles with an integral anchor, to the NAAMM Metal Bar Grating Manual, with shop drawings and manufacturer's instructions as the submittal (the paragraph asks for a "dimensional layout of all handrailing," as written) [¶1.01 A, ¶1.02 A, ¶1.03 A, p.205 — Verified]. Solid plank grating is for UV channels only: 2" all-aluminum slip-resistant plank (SlipNOT or equal) that blocks all light from leaving the UV channel, on embedment angles per the grating manufacturer [¶2.01 A–C, p.205 — Verified]. Aluminum bar grating carries 100 psf at L/360 (2.0" deep, 4.0 lb/sf for 2'-6" to 6'-7" spans), is banded I-bar, and sits flush on 2"×2"×1/4" gray pultruded FRP embedment angles (IKG or equal) [¶2.02 A–C, p.205 — Verified]. Grating is fastened with removable aluminum clips and Type 316 stainless bolts, with galvanic isolation and bituminous paint where aluminum meets concrete or grout [¶3.01 A–C, p.206 — Verified]; S2.2 instead supports the grating on HDG steel angles and calls solid plank grating as typical at both basins [plans_8 p.2, Details 5–6 — Verified-Visual; Unresolved — Issues, Conflicts].

The section does not cover the R-10 XPS footing-perimeter insulation or the Dryvit EIFS shown on A1.2, and 02 lists no other Division 07 section except 07 92 00 [¶1.1 A, p.209; plans_7 p.6 — Verified-Visual; 02 index — Verified; Unresolved — Issues, Missing information].

Add. 4 adds Service Condition J for the new Blower Building gypsum board: one coat Sherwin-Williams wall primer (DF 2.0) and two coats Sherwin-Williams Pro-Mar alkyd egg-shell (DF 4.0) [Add. 4 p.2, 09 90 00 ¶2.01 I — Verified; Add. 4 governs]. Execution requires concrete cured 28 days before painting, galvanized surfaces passivated before priming, and exposed piping painted and labeled by service (for example, 2W light blue, air white) with a labeling walk-through before Substantial Completion [¶3.02 G and L, pp.233–234; ¶3.05, p.235 — Verified].

- **Summary:** Covers a spray-applied, monolithic, high-build 100%-solids epoxy lining on all interior concrete surfaces of the listed structure — the Influent Pump Station, with detailed locations per the drawings [¶1.01 A–B, p.236 — Verified]. Submittals are product data with ASTM results, MSDS, project guidelines and applicator qualifications (manufacturer certification, trained equipment operators, 5 references, permits) [¶1.02 A, p.236 — Verified]. The product is Raven 405 or Tnemec G436 PermaShield (amine-cured, light green), applied by a certified applicator with heated plural-component spray on concrete cured at least 28 days and blasted to CSP 3–5, at 80 mils min. and 100 mils average wet film [¶2.01 A, ¶2.04 A, ¶2.05, ¶3.03 E, ¶3.05 D, pp.237–239 — Verified]. Testing covers wet-film readings (ASTM D4414), optional ultrasonic dry-film checks (SSPC-PA 9), high-voltage holiday testing at 100 V/mil (NACE RP0188 or ASTM D4787), and pull-off adhesion (ASTM D7234) with 2 of 3 pulls over 200 psi per test area, all documented to the Owner [¶3.06 A–D, pp.239–241 — Verified].

### 10 73 05 — FRP Launder Covers

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 55

Document ID: 10 73 05 | Type: Specification section, 3 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical (lane per 02, decision D) | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: The contractor furnishes and installs a molded, UV-protected, opaque FRP cover over the Train 3 clarifier weir launder and weir, in curved sections at least 4 ft long, each a single section on two pivoting brackets opening toward the tank center, with spring-loaded latches, an optional stainless tether, fixed or vertical panels at the bridge support, no piano hinges, no inspection hatches, and not walk-on except for a reinforced walk-on section at the launder entry (¶1.01 A–H pp.243–244). Panels are isophthalic polyester with UV suppression, at least 35% glass by weight (ASTM D2584-18), nominal 1/4" thick and no more than 55 lb each, with tensile 26,000 psi, flexural 30,000 psi, flexural modulus 1.9×10^6 psi, Barcol 50, notched Izod 20 ft-lb/in and water absorption 0.25% max; fasteners, handles, hinges and latches are stainless (¶3.01 B–G pp.244–245). Submittals per 01 33 00 are catalog data and O&M information (¶2.01 A p.244); the contractor adds a 1-year parts-and-labor extended warranty from substantial completion (¶1.02 A p.244); three sets of wear parts are furnished (¶3.02 p.245); and the manufacturer provides installation certification, 2 days of start-up and field testing and 1 day of operator training (¶4.02 p.245). ¶3.01 A, under the launder cover heading, repeats the packaged sludge pump paragraph of 22 13 36 ¶2.01 A word for word (Edson peristaltic pumps, 3 HP, a VFD per pump) — Issue 20. The section runs in four parts (Part 2 submittals, Part 3 products, Part 4 execution).

- **Summary:** Covers a rigid-frame, bolted, pre-manufactured metal building with galvanized cold-formed secondary members and pre-coated metal roofing and siding, for the Blower Building and the existing building modifications shown on A1.3 [¶1.01 A–B, p.246 — Verified]. Garco Building Systems or equal (AISC Category MB certified, MBMA member) supplies 50 ksi hot-dip galvanized primary framing, G-90 16-gauge secondary members, A325 bolts, galvanized cable or rod bracing, 24-gauge Galvalume standing-seam roof panels (UL 90) and 26-gauge Galvalume R- or A-type wall panels, factory-finished in polyester or Kynar at the Owner's choice [¶1.05, ¶2.01–¶2.04, pp.247–249 — Verified; Add. 4 p.1 Clarification 3 — Verified]. ¶2.05 A.1 describes the Blower Building as a rigid frame of "42' bldg. width, 9.75' bldg. length" with 9'-0" minimum haunch clearance, which Add. 4 supersedes with a 44.00' × 9.25' footprint and 10.25' clearance [p.249 — Verified; Add. 4 p.1 Clarification 4, p.4, p.5 — Verified; Add. 4 governs]. Installers need 5 years' experience, anchor bolts and base plates are verified against the approved erection drawings, bolts are tightened by turn-of-nut (preferred), and panels are lapped and butyl-sealed with trim and closures for a weather-tight enclosure; the section has no testing article [¶3.01–¶3.03, pp.250–251 — Verified].

Document ID: 15 40 00 | Type: Specification section, 2 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical (lane per 02, decision D) | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: Covers miscellaneous plumbing "for exterior purposes only" as described and shown on the plans (¶1.01 A p.253), with shop drawing and product data submittals (¶1.02 A) and compliance with the UBC, UPC and Eastsound Sewer and Water District standards (¶1.03 A). Materials: ABS DWV soil and waste piping above and below ground, unions at equipment connections, full-size cleanouts ("provide brass lugs, with graphite lubricant on threads"), 1" frost-proof yard hydrants (stainless rod and pipe, automatic draining, backflow preventer, 3/4" hose thread, Smith Cooper 0190167II vacuum breaker or equal, 4' bury; Woodford Iowa Y2 or Merrill CV-1000 or equal), and 2" post hydrants (316 SS hardware, 304 SS lower stem rod, hand wheel, brass working parts, 4' bury; American AVK Dry Barrel 67XX or Eclipse No. 2A or equal) with 2" Danfoss AVB atmospheric vacuum breakers (125 psi, ASSE 1001) (¶2.01 A–E p.253). Installation slopes drains 1/4" per foot, provides code cleanouts and follows manufacturer instructions and local codes (¶3.01 p.254); no testing is specified. No sheet in the P&M stack shows hydrant or drain locations or quantities (cross-lane item for Merge).

### 22 13 29 — Submersible Sewerage Pumps

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 59

Document ID: 22 13 29 | Type: Specification section, 8 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); running header misspells the title ("SUMBERSIBLE") — already logged in 02 (#7)
Summary: A performance specification for the complete, tested Influent Pump Station — pumps, controls, discharge piping, vaults, flow meter and connection to existing piping — with the contractor responsible for field measurements and supplier coordination (¶1.01 A–E p.255). Pump schedule (¶2.02 B p.258): two small pumps, Flygt N3085 MT, 150 gpm @ 20.5 ft design (120 gpm @ 22.0 ft; 220 gpm @ 17.5 ft; shutoff 26 ft), 2.7 HP, 1800 rpm, 43% minimum efficiency, 135 mm Adaptive N-impeller, 3 1/8" discharge; two large pumps, Flygt N3102 MT, 385 gpm @ 20.5 ft (320 gpm @ 22.5 ft; 500 gpm @ 15.0 ft; shutoff 38 ft), 5.0 HP, 1800 rpm, 66%, 152 mm impeller, 3 15/16" discharge; all 460 V 3-phase, rated Class 1 Division 1 Groups C and D, one manufacturer for all (¶2.01 C; ¶2.02 C.1, C.9 pp.257–260). Each pump has a cast-iron discharge connection with a machined metal-to-metal seal, two 316 SS guide bars about 15 ft long (contractor to verify), 316 SS brackets and a 20 ft stainless lifting chain with spectra cord for a Grip-Eye device, three 316 SS cable holders, a Flygt MiniCAS-120 motor saver relay supplied to the Control Systems Integrator, and pressure gages (Marsh P0142, 0–25 psi) with Branom diaphragm isolators and Apollo shutoff valves (¶2.02 C.2, C.10, F; ¶2.03; ¶2.04 pp.259–261). Submittals include certified curves, shop drawings of the station and lifting system, control panel and interconnection wiring, warranty and factory test report (¶1.03 A p.256); testing includes certified non-witnessed factory performance tests to Hydraulic Institute standards, a factory operational test of the completed station submitted before installation, field tests with the manufacturer's representative, and at least one 8-hour start-up day with flow tests (¶1.03 C, ¶1.04 B–C p.256; ¶3.03 p.262). The warranty is 5 years or 10,000 hours (¶1.05 A p.256), and float switches hang at the plan levels with a submersible level transducer also used for pump control (¶3.02 A p.261).
Tags: Equipment — influent pumps (2 small, 2 large), discharge connections, guide bars, lifting chains, cable holders, motor saver relays, pressure gages, isolators, shutoff valves, float switches, level transducer · Spec sections — 01 33 00, 01 50 00, 03 30 00, 31 23 33, 33 05 00, 33 31 00, Division 26 (related, ¶1.02) · Sheets — "as shown on the Drawings" (none numbered) · Addenda — none
Related documents: C4.1; G0.7; 04 Equipment Alternate A (Flygt base; spec link now matches by manufacturer).

### 22 13 36 — Packaged Sludge Pumps

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 60

Document ID: 22 13 36 | Type: Specification section, 2 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); ¶2.01 A revised by Add. 4 (February 9, 2023) p.2 — Add. 4 governs
Summary: Furnish and install three sludge pumps — one WAS pump, one digested sludge pump and one swing pump for either service — for dilute sludge at 0.5% to 3.0% solids (¶1.01 A–B p.263). Base ¶2.01 A (p.263) calls for Edson Platinum peristaltic pumps or equal, 3 HP, 460 V, 3-phase, 0 to 43 gpm (typical set point 10 to 30 gpm), corrosion resistant, with a VFD and a local speed dial per pump, an electrical control panel per pump and a 304 SS enclosure of about 40"×40"×40" per pump. Add. 4 p.2 replaces "Each pump is to include a VFD" with: pumps powered from VFDs in the Motor Control Center, VFDs controlled by the PLC in the Main Control Panel, no local speed control dial at the pump, see Electrical Sheets — Add. 4 governs. Controls follow 26 80 00 ¶3.09 A (WAS pump control) and H (rotary fan press system) (¶2.02 p.263); submittals per 01 33 00 are catalog data with wiring diagrams and O&M information (¶1.02 A p.263); the manufacturer furnishes three sets of wear parts (¶2.03 p.263) and provides installation certification, 2 days of start-up and field testing and 1 day of operator training (¶3.02 p.264).
Tags: Equipment — WAS pump, digested sludge pump, swing pump, local control panels (3), pump enclosures (3) · Spec sections — 01 33 00; 26 80 00 ¶3.09 A, H · Sheets — none numbered · Addenda — Add. 4 p.2, 22 13 36 ¶2.01 A
