### 23 34 00 — Wall Fans and Louvered Vents

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 61

Document ID: 23 34 00 | Type: Specification section, 3 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical (lane per 02, decision D; cross-check E9.3 and A sheets) | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: Scope (¶1.01 A p.265): in the Blower Building, two 3,300 CFM wall fans on individual thermostats, one motorized 36"×84" louvered vent that opens when either fan runs and stays open until both are off, and one manual 24"×24" louvered vent; in the "Existing Dewatering/Train 1&2 Building Area", three 3,300 CFM wall fans on individual manual on/off switches and two manual 36"×84" louvered vents. Fans are Acme FQ18G6 (3,300 CFM, 1/2 HP, 120 V 1-phase, 12 sones max) with wall collar and guard, each with a Louvers & Dampers EBE445 aluminum gravity combination louver (about 24"×24") with stainless bird screen and a 2-year minimum manufacturer warranty; thermostats are Schaffer TH109 two-speed, 40–100°F, in NEMA 4X enclosures with stainless coil sensors, 4' above floor (¶1.01 B; ¶2.1 A–B pp.265–266). Vents are "Pottoroff" (as printed) EXD-645 combination louver/dampers with drainable blades, vinyl seals, washable 18×16 stainless insect screens and non-protruding actuation; the motorized unit uses a Pottoroff AF120 120 V spring-return actuator driven from the fan thermostat auxiliary contacts (¶2.2–2.3 pp.266–267). ¶2.1 A.4 calls all wall fans dual speed and thermostat controlled, while ¶1.01 A puts the three existing-building fans on manual switches (Issue 25). The section has no submittal or testing paragraph; installation follows the manufacturer's instructions, with controls 4' above floor (¶3.1 p.267).
Tags: Equipment — Blower Building wall fans (2) and thermostats (2), motorized louvered vent 36"×84", manual louvered vent 24"×24"; existing building wall fans (3) and switches, manual louvered vents 36"×84" (2); gravity combination louvers (5) · Spec sections — none cited · Sheets — "shown on the drawings" (none numbered) · Addenda — none cited (Add. 4 p.3 moves "Exhaust Fans" with the Blower Building layout; link Inferred)

Testing is witnessed with 48-hour notice: force mains at 1.5 times operating pressure or 150 psi for 15 minutes (mixed liquor and West Plant Drain Pump Station force mains 150 psi, 2W and 3W piping 200 psi), HDPE per ASTM F2164, and valves with their pipelines (¶3.04.1–6, 8, 10; pp.427–429 — Verified). Summary: One rising-stem 4" telescoping valve with at least 48" of travel on the aerated digester outlet, as indicated on the plans (¶1.01 A–B p.451). Construction: seamless stainless tube at least 1/8" thick on a stainless lifting bail; stainless companion flange with a neoprene slip-seal gasket at least 1/4" thick (ID 1/8" under the tube OD), 125# drilling to match the riser; handwheel lift at least 12" in diameter with UHMW thrust bearings, a self-locking 304 SS acme stem, a galvanized square-tube torque nut (or vee-keyed shaft) against rotation, and a clear butyrate stem cover with a position indicator in 1/4" increments; 316 SS pedestal on the concrete side wall with six 5/8"×5" stainless wedge anchors (¶2.01 pp.451–452). Basis of design is Waterman TS-2 or equal (¶2.02 p.452); submittals per 01 33 00 are catalog data, fabrication and installation drawings, and O&M information (¶1.02 A p.451); installation follows the manufacturer's instructions and no testing is specified (¶3.01 p.452). The 48" travel matches the 21.3–25.3 T-valve operating range on C5.3 (Inferred check).

### 41 12 13 — Screw Conveyor System

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 92

Document ID: 41 12 13 | Type: Specification section, 9 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: One complete screw conveyor system carries dewatered cake "from the rotary presses" to the final discharge point, with drives, motors, instrumentation, controls and supports, designed by the manufacturer to CEMA, AGMA, AWS D1.1 and AISC standards (¶1.1–1.2, ¶1.5 pp.453–455). The schedule (pp.460–461) lists two units: a "Receiving Conveyor" (horizontal, shaftless, 9" flight at 9" pitch, 6'-9" long, 304 SS U-trough 11 ga, two inlets from rotary press cake chutes, outlet to the inclined conveyor, Nord SK2282 SCP, 1 HP, 24 rpm) and an "Inclined Conveyor" (shafted, 9" flight at 5" pitch, 17'-4" long at 35°, 304 SS tubular O-trough 10 ga, outlet to bin, Nord SK2282 SCP, 5 HP, 53 rpm), both 0.5 wet ton/hr at 45% loading and 45 lb/ft³, with 3/8" UHMW liners and 2" drains. Troughs follow CEMA 300 in sections of 8 ft max, with UHMW wear liners in 4-ft pieces, a 2" stainless ball drain valve, anti-flotation devices every 16 ft on shaftless units and saddle supports at 16 ft max; drives are TEFC inverter-duty 1800 rpm motors on Nord shaft-mount reducers (AGMA Class 11, 1.4 service factor), with Siemens Sitrans WM100 zero-speed switches (120 VAC, NEMA 4X) wired to the PLC and interlocked with the MCC starter, and the conveyors run from the rotary press PLC and operator interface (¶1.8–1.13 pp.456–459). Submittals cite "Section 01340" and "Section 01 30 00" and repeat telescoping valve wording (¶1.3 A p.454; Issue 23), plus data sheets and component lists (¶1.3 B); each conveyor is shop assembled and run before shipment (¶3.02 A p.460). The two-conveyor, two-inlet arrangement does not match C6.1's single 28° conveyor from one press (Issue 22); Part 2 numbering runs 1.6–1.13 as printed.

### 43 11 00 — Blower Equipment

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 93

Document ID: 43 11 00 | Type: Specification section, 10 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); pages numbered 431100-1 to -10 — already logged in 02 (#9)
Summary: Four identical Kaeser Com-paK DB 166 C positive-displacement blower packages (20 HP, 460 V 3-phase, VFD variable speed) serve the Biological Treatment System from the Blower Building, and one Kaeser Com-paK BB 52 C (5 HP, VFD) serves the aerobic digester from the existing metal building just north of the proposed blower building, "just west of" and "adjacent to" Train #2; Kaeser, Aerzen or approved equal (¶1.01 A–B p.462; ¶2.01 A pp.462–463; ¶O p.469). Design points: 315 scfm at 8.9 psig differential per biological blower (325 icfm, 10.1 psig max, relief at 12.0 psig, 17.4 bhp, 3,999 rpm, 207°F discharge) and 120 scfm at 4.0 psig for the digester blower (124 icfm, 6.6 psig max, relief at 8.0 psig, 3.8 bhp), each in a 72 dB(A) sound enclosure (¶2.01 A; ¶P p.470). Packages are oil-free tri-lobe and belt-driven with automatic belt tensioning, integral inlet filter-silencer and discharge silencer, spring relief valve, flexible connector, pressure, temperature and filter differential gauges, TEFC IP-55 motors with PTC thermistors at 18–60 Hz, 100,000-hour L10 bearings and vibration to VDI 3836 (¶2.01 B–K pp.463–467). Each blower is slip tested and run 30 minutes at maximum speed and differential, each package runs 15 minutes before shipment with a conformance certificate, the blower warranty is 60 months from shipment (other components 1 year from substantial completion), and submittals per 01 33 00 are catalog data, fabrication and installation drawings, and O&M information (¶2.01 L–N pp.467–468; ¶1.02 A p.462). Controls sit in a NEMA 12 cabinet with the VFDs in the MCC, and the contractor wires the over-temperature, over-pressure and motor temperature sensors to the PLC control panel and supplies tees, supports and fittings (¶2.01 Q, ¶3.01 A p.471).

### 43 22 10 — 2W Plant Water System Equipment

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 94

Document ID: 43 22 10 | Type: Specification section, 4 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); ¶2.01 A revised by Add. 4 (February 9, 2023) p.2 — Add. 4 governs
Summary: A complete 2W plant water system: two 2W pumps, pump station accessories, one fiberglass enclosure with a flow meter system, one fiberglass enclosure with an air gap system, two air diaphragm tanks, yard hydrants, wall-mount eye wash stations, and the piping and valving shown (¶1.01 A–B p.472). Base ¶2.01 A (p.472): two ITT Goulds 55GS50 4"-well submersible pumps with stainless components and Centripro M50434 5 HP 460 V 3-phase motors (or equal), 20–80 gpm, "Pumps are to include variable frequency drives (VFDs)", holding a constant user-set pressure (about 50–75 psi) and alternating after each on/off cycle; Add. 4 p.2 replaces the VFD sentence with VFDs in the Motor Control Center controlled by the PLC in the Main Control Panel — Add. 4 governs. Accessories are Orenco SBEX splice boxes, SBCS conduit seals, an MF2 float switch assembly with MFCOLLAR1 collars, HV discharge assemblies (2" with ball valve, check valve, flex hose and quick disconnect per pump), a PB30 pump basin and pressure relief valves each set at 110 psi (Clayton Mark or equal) (¶2.02 A p.473); the tanks are Amtrol Well-X-Trol WX-350D, 119 gal (26" × 62"), with relief valves at 100 psi on the tank tee and 120 psi on the pump discharge piping, a further 120 psi relief valve, a pressure transducer for pump on/off and a 0–150 psi gauge (¶2.03 A p.473; Issue 26). Exposed plant water pipe gets 1.25" closed-cell polyisocyanurate insulation (Trymer 2000 XP or equal) under a 26-gauge aluminum jacket, and exposed plant water piping, hydrants and pump discharge piping are labeled and painted per "Section 09 91 00" and Divisions 9 and 33, stainless pipe excepted (¶2.04–2.05 pp.473–474; Issue 27). Submittals per 01 33 00 are catalog data, fabrication and installation drawings and O&M information (¶1.02 A p.472); one set of special tools is furnished (¶1.03 A); installation includes verifying pump rotation, and the suppliers train the Owner (¶3.01–3.02 p.475).

Summary: Covers furnishing and installing the valves in the plant piping systems, complete with operators, with submittals per 01 33 00 and NSF 61 materials for potable water (¶1.01–2.01 p.476). ### 46 33 33 — Emulsion Polymer Make-up System

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 96

Document ID: 46 33 33 | Type: Specification section, 5 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: One fully operational emulsion (liquid) polymer make-up system that activates and blends neat polymer (40–55% active) into a homogeneous 0.1–0.5% solution (¶1.01 A p.481). Components: a water pressure reducing valve set to 40 psi (supply 45–100 psi) with gauge; a proportional dilution-water valve (ball valve and positioner needing at least 90 psi air; flow "0.1 to 20 GPM (0.4 to 75 LPH)" as printed — Issue 29) regulated on the polymer-to-sludge ratio from the Rotary Press PLC, using the flow meter on the flocculator supplied with the press; a peristaltic metering pump (0.1–500 mL/min, up to 7 bar) with an inlet strainer; and a static mixer, on a 304 SS frame with HDPE water piping (Part 2 items 2.1–2.5 pp.481–482). Electrical: a 24 VDC, 4–20 mA proportional valve, a 120 VAC or 24 VDC pump with 4–20 mA control, and a NEMA 4X junction box with a 120 VAC/24 VDC supply, powered from a 120 VAC outlet or the Fournier control panel and run from the Rotary Press PLC and HMI in hand or auto (items 3.0–4.0 p.482). The materials list names Fournier Industries (frame, brackets), Watson-Marlow (dosing pump), Koflo (static mixer), Watts (regulator), Festo (proportional valve), Hoffman (10×10×8 junction box) and McMaster (valves, strainer, gauges, hose), with one metering pump head kit as the spare (items 6.0–7.0 pp.482–483); p.484 carries no text. Submittals cite "Section 01340" with telescoping-valve wording (¶1.02 A p.481; Issue 29), installation follows the manufacturer's instructions with no testing specified (¶3.01 p.485), and the liquid system conflicts with C6.1's "dry polymer mix unit" label (Issue 16).

### 46 41 23 — Submersible Mixers

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 97

Document ID: 46 41 23 | Type: Specification section, 5 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: Furnish and install two mixer assemblies, two masts and two davit cranes, with one mixer in each anoxic zone of new Train #3 (¶1.01 A p.486). Mixers are at most 5 HP nameplate, 460 V 3-phase, with 316 Ti stainless propeller (EverClean blades), shaft, housing and frame, a duplex stainless casing cover, silicon carbide double mechanical seals in an oil chamber, a plug-and-receptacle power cable with resin-sealed entry, three PTC thermal switches and a moisture detector, bearings over 100,000 hours B10, maintenance intervals of at least 16,000 hours or 2 years, and 10 starts per hour (¶2.01 A–B, F pp.486–489). Each mixer rides a permanent 4"-square stainless guide mast with sockets at 10 ft max, a 14-position rotary top plate and an adjustable stop bracket (or a floor mounting pedestal), and two portable stainless lifting davits with manual brake winches mount on the top mast bracket, with removable handrail or chain sections so mixers need not be lifted over the handrail (¶2.01 C–E p.488); the mixer supplier furnishes motor-sensor monitoring relays that others install in the motor control panel (¶2.01 B.4 p.487). Controls follow 26 80 00 ¶3.09 B (¶2.02 p.489); submittals per 01 33 00 are catalog data with wiring diagrams and O&M information (¶1.02 A p.486); the contractor gives a 1-year parts-and-labor extended warranty (¶1.03 A); three sets of wear parts are furnished (¶2.03); and testing covers factory checks (balance, leakage, electrical, insulation, no-load rotation), field testing after installation, 2 days of start-up and 1 day of training (¶2.04 p.489; ¶3.01 B–3.02 p.490). The same two mixers are also specified in 46 53 00, whose single-source and precedence clauses overlap this section (Issue 30).

Document ID: 46 51 21 | Type: Specification section, 1 page | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: Furnish and install coarse bubble diffusers as indicated on the plans; the only location listed is the aerobic digester (¶1.01 A–B p.491). Diffusers are 24" long, 316 SS, clog-free, open-bottom, with a rubber check valve in the air inlet against backflow of liquid or sludge, by Aquarius Technologies, SSI Aeration or approved equal, and removable from above the water line without entering the basin (¶2.01 A–B p.491). Submittals per 01 33 00 are catalog data, fabrication and installation drawings coordinated with piping connections, and O&M information (¶1.02 A p.491); the contractor supplies tees, supports and fittings, installation follows the manufacturer's instructions, and no testing is specified (¶3.01 p.491). No quantity is given; G0.7 lists 6 diffusers at 20 cfm (Verified, plans_1 p.7).

### 46 53 00 — Biological Treatment System

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 99

Document ID: 46 53 00 | Type: Specification section, 10 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: Specifies Train 3 ("3rd Train") as a single bullseye multistage anoxic/aerobic activated sludge system, basis of design Smith & Loveless Model R Oxigest, with the in-basin aeration system, mixers, clarifier equipment, instrumentation and controls supplied and guaranteed by one manufacturer (¶1.01 A p.492; ¶1.03 A p.493); this section's valves, equipment, materials and controls supersede other specifications (¶1.03 F p.493), and alternate systems are bid as an Equipment Alternate with a submittal package (¶1.03 E, ¶1.04 p.493). Design influent is 63,333 gpd average to 178 gpm peak hour, with effluent BOD5 and TSS 30 mg/L and TIN 8 mg/L (¶1.02 p.492); the supplier gives a 2-year process performance guarantee with the equipment submittal and a 12-month mechanical guarantee from Substantial Completion (¶1.05 A, C p.494). The tank has a reinforced concrete outer wall and structural steel plate inner walls of at least 1/4" (¶2.01 B.1 p.494), 32 ft ID, 15.5 ft static depth and 16.9 ft overall wall height, 43,000 gal of annular aeration and 26,508 gal of anoxic volume in two compartments, each with a 5 HP 460 V submersible mixer, and 240 scfm through S&L medium bubble diffusers plus 75 scfm of airlift air, 315 scfm total (¶2.01 C.1 p.495; header and drop pipes ¶2.01 C.2 p.496). The 18 ft clarifier has 1.4 ft freeboard, a 1:12 concrete floor placed by the purchaser, an 800 ft-lb drive with a 1/2 HP 460 V motor, a scum airlift discharging to the aerobic digester and drive-failure alarm contacts; the system also includes a 3'-0" walkway, a 2'-0" service walkway, aluminum grating, handrail and ladder, a RAS airlift (51 gpm at 15 cfm, 3" riser), MLR airlifts (two at 101 gpm and 30 cfm, 6" risers) and carbon feed by two Stenner S3V02AA302N peristaltic pumps, 1 duty and 1 standby (¶2.01 B.2–B.3 p.495; ¶2.01 C.3–C.8 pp.496–498). Blowers are in 43 11 00 and air piping in 33 31 00 ¶2.01 D, the air header gets a 15 psi, 60-minute low-pressure air test (¶2.01 D–E p.498), and the manufacturer furnishes a NEMA 4X 316 SS control panel and spares and performs programming, start-up and a witnessed Functional Acceptance Test (¶2.01 F–G p.499; ¶3.03 pp.500–501).
Tags: Equipment — Train 3 system package, inner steel walls and partitions, clarifier mechanism, walkways and ladder, air header and diffusers, submersible mixers (2), DO sensors, RAS airlift, MLR airlifts, scum airlift, carbon feed pumps (2), treatment system control panel · Spec sections — 43 11 00; 33 31 00 ¶2.01 D; 26 80 00 ¶3.09 C · Sheets — "as shown on the drawings" (no sheet numbers cited) · Addenda — none

### 46 66 00 — UV Equipment

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 100

Document ID: 46 66 00 | Type: Specification section, 7 pages (body title "Ultraviolet Disinfection Equipment") | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: Two open-channel, gravity-flow, low-pressure high-intensity UV systems, each able to disinfect alone at max day 0.46 MGD, max month 0.22 MGD and minimum 0.03 MGD with TSS up to 30 mg/L, 33–85°F and UVT of at least 50% at 253.7 nm, to 200 FC/100 mL monthly and 400 FC/100 mL weekly geometric means, guaranteed regardless of influent count (¶1.01 A p.502; design criteria pp.502–503). Arrangement: 4 lamps per module, 6 modules per bank, 2 banks, 48 lamps (12 modules), in stainless channels furnished with the system, delivering at least 30,000 µWs/cm² (30 mJ/cm²) at 50% UVT after sleeve, fouling and aging reductions, proven by an independent bioassay to EPA/625/1-86/021, for outdoor installation without shelter (pp.503–504); basis is Trojan UV3000 PTP (base bid), with alternates bid as an Equipment Alternate and needing 500 North American installations, a bioassay and a 5-year guarantee (¶1.03 A p.502; Part 2 A p.504). Construction: 316 SS or quartz in contact with effluent; modules about 68"×20"×2.8", 38 lb, Type 6P, with electronic ballasts, 10-ft 120 V plug cords and LED lamp status; low-pressure slimline hot-cathode lamps warranted 12,000 hours non-prorated; Type 214 quartz sleeves; per-unit effluent channel with drain, module rack and downstream level control weir holding about 12" depth within 1.5" from zero to peak flow; two inlet and two outlet 304 SS transition boxes; a 304 SS maintenance rack; maximum draw 2,100 W at 120 V (pp.505–507). Monitoring: two submersible UV intensity sensors (one per bank, 10-year warranty), intensity and lamp-age displays and a low-UV dry contact in a fiberglass Type 4X wall panel less than 12 ft from the module LED end, plus GFCI duplex PDRs by the UV manufacturer, with the contractor supplying a 120 V circuit of about 19.2 A and a separate 120 V 5 A monitoring supply (pp.506–507). Submittals include shop drawings, electrical schematics, hydraulic calculations, bioassay and dose calculations and a lifetime performance guarantee (¶1.04 pp.503–504); equipment warranty is 12 months from substantial completion (¶1.05 p.504); spares are 4 lamps, 4 sleeves, 4 seals and an operator kit per bank (p.507); and the manufacturer provides 2 days of start-up and field testing and 1 day of training (¶3.02 p.508).
Tags: Equipment — UV systems (2), UV modules (12), lamps (48), stainless channels and level control weirs, transition boxes (4), maintenance rack, UV intensity sensors (2), monitoring panel, PDRs · Spec sections — 03 30 00; Division 26 (related) · Sheets — "as shown on the drawings" (none numbered) · Addenda — none
Related documents: C5.1–C5.4; G0.6, G0.7; 46 66 10; 04 Equipment Alternate D (Trojan base, confirmed here). Issues 13, 32, 33.

### 46 76 26 — Rotary Fan Press System

> Merge: lane — Process & Mechanical · Issue references → Process_and_Mechanical_Issues.md · index — 02 row 102

Document ID: 46 76 26 | Type: Specification section, 22 pages | Discipline: Process-Mechanical | Revision/Date: main spec issue Dec. 30, 2022 (per 00); no section-level date
Summary: A single-source, shop-tested "plug and play" dewatering skid: one rotary fan press with one 36" dewatering channel, a flocculator, sludge manifold and piping, cake chute, an emulsified-polymer make-up system with dosing pump, an air compressor for pneumatic valving, screw conveyors (receiving and inclined "as needed" to reach the District's dumpster, as shown on drawings) and one control panel for the whole system, which also sets the digested sludge feed pump rate (¶1.0 A, C p.510; ¶1.7 p.514). Duty: at least 120 dry lb/hr from 0.5–2.5% solids at 5–25 gpm, producing 12–16% cake and up to 1,000 wet lb/hr, with design runtimes of 4.6–35.0 hours per week across current and 2040 cases (¶1.0 B, D, E pp.510–511); the press has a 1.5 HP 480 V TEFC drive, stainless hard-chrome filtration elements, six scrapers, a wash system limited to 750 gallons per 2.5 hours because "the 2W (air gap) water system has limited capacity", an LLDPE filtrate collector (7.5" outlet) to a 6" Sch 80 PVC manifold and LLDPE cake chutes (¶1.1–1.4 pp.512–514). The flocculator (model 05-300-900, 17 gal, 100 psi) has a 1 HP Nord SK0282 variable-speed agitator and a PVC air breather tied to "the potable water line", feeding the press through Sch 10 316 SS piping with Victaulic joints, 3" pneumatic feed and recirculation ball valves, lever channel isolating valves, 1" pneumatic wash valves and a 1" stainless polymer check valve, with the feed manifold shop tested at 100 psi (¶2.1–2.2 pp.515–518). Instruments and controls: E+H 10W 3" sludge and 10P 1" polymer electromagnetic flowmeters and a PMC71 0–30 psi pressure transmitter at the flocculator inlet, per-channel cake sensors and outlet air regulators, a wash-water pressure switch, and a NEMA 12, 480 V, CSA control cabinet with an Allen-Bradley CompactLogix 5370 PLC, PanelView Plus 7 HMI, PowerFlex 52X VFDs, an eWON Cosy 141 modem, a NEMA 4X press junction box and E-stop, programmed with the listed alarms, shutdowns and screens (¶4.1–4.4 pp.520–527). Submittals cover certified shop and erection drawings, electrical schematics and I/O diagrams, bills of material, spare parts lists and a detailed O&M manual (¶5.0–5.2 pp.527–530); the warranty is 1 year non-prorated from substantial completion (¶6.1 pp.530–531); there is no field start-up, field testing or training paragraph (Issue 37); and ¶1.1 cites "Section 22 13 16" for both the screw conveyor and the sludge pump (Issue 35).
Tags: Equipment — rotary fan press, flocculator, sludge feed manifold and valves, cake chute and sensor, filtrate collector and manifold, air compressor, polymer system, screw conveyors, sludge and polymer flowmeters, inlet pressure transmitter, control cabinet (PLC, HMI, VFDs, modem), junction box · Spec sections — 41 12 13, 46 33 33, 22 13 36; "22 13 16" (as printed); 01 33 00 · Sheets — "as shown on drawings" (none numbered) · Addenda — none (Add. 4 22 13 36 item bears on the feed pump; Issue 36)

### QA plan — Construction Quality Assurance Plan (Wilson Engineering)

> Merge: lane — Contract & General · Issue references → Contract_and_General_Issues.md · index — 00 register row 4

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Document ID | CQA Plan (file `cqa-plan-eswd-wwtp-ph1-2022-12-01.pdf`; 00 register row 4) |
| Type | Reference document — Engineer's construction quality assurance plan; states it is not part of the Contract Documents (§1.3, p.5) |
| Discipline | General |
| Revision/Date | December 1, 2022; Wilson Engineering Project # 2020-070 (p.1) |
| Summary | Wilson Engineering's plan, prepared under WAC 173-240-075, for documenting that construction meets the contract plans and specifications; it says it is not part of the Contract Documents, so the contract governs wherever the two differ (§1.0–1.3, pp.4–5; Verified; WAC and WSDOT 2022 texts Unresolved: external reference, not staged). The District (General Manager Jason Bradshaw) funds the work, approves pay requests and changes, inspects daily and must keep treatment uninterrupted; Wilson (Project Engineer Jeff Christner, PE; Inspector Sam Rodriguez; subconsultants Ztek electrical, GeoEngineers geotechnical, Kingworks structural) performs CQA, submittal review, record drawings and the plant O&M manual; the WA-licensed Contractor owns quality control (§4, pp.6–9; Fig. 4-1 p.23; Verified; rule citations — Issues 2.10). Wilson observes on site at least 1 day per week and keeps daily reports, a weekly working-day tally, photos and quantity records, and the plan expects a Contractor field office to hold the schedule, photos, pay requests, test records, RFI/Work Directive/Change Order files and reviewed submittals (§5, §8, pp.10–12, p.18; Verified; field office — Issues 1.16). Owner-paid QA covers earthwork sieve and compaction checks and concrete slump, air and temperature on each initial load plus four cylinders per mix per day or per 150 CY, whichever is less; Contractor QC covers sieve analyses by an independent certified lab for non-structural items, leakage testing per 01 50 00 ¶1.04.C, start-up per 01 91 00 with the Engineer present, and all pipe testing with the Inspector present (§7, pp.16–18; Verified; sieve-analysis split — Issues 1.24). Changes run by RFI, Work Directive and Change Order signed by Owner, Engineer and Contractor, with Ecology review before any change order that departs significantly from the plans and specs; Figure 3-1 repeats the 00 31 13 schedule, adds Advertisement No. 2 (Jan 11), Contract Date (Mar 1) and NTP (Mar 15, 2023), and labels the construction phase "October 2022 – April 2024" (§9, pp.19–20; pp.21–22; Verified; Issues 1.10, 1.25, 1.26). |
